Owner: Tyler.
  - D-153 The default-Off web build, per sheet: what it delivers, how a sync-only v2 project is found, and the scope of the gate (requirements.md "The classic file side on the web", blocks `classic-web-file-side`, `classic-web-scenarios`, `v2-discovery`, `web-value`; Tasks 1.4.7o, 1.4.7n; product P1, P2, P3; owner-confirmable; amends D-147): D-147 said a project that holds a v2 sheet is reconciled regardless, which (a) could not find a project created on desktop and synced by git, because the web database has no row for it and knowing that it holds a v2 sheet means reading the bundle, which is the gated file side, so the headline claim "a v2 sheet synced from desktop opens editable" needed the setting `On`, and (b) would have turned the whole classic file side on for the classic sheets of a mixed project. The gate now decides per SHEET: under `Off` only v2 sheets have a file side and the classic sheets of a mixed project stay database-only; and a bounded, read-only discovery scan (`SyncedV2ProjectDiscovery`, Task 1.4.7n) finds the projects of the sidecar root that the database does not know: at graph open, after the preload, for at most 200 unknown projects within 500 ms, it lists `sheets/` from the preloaded listing and reads the first 256 bytes of ONE `*.v2.json` file through the header classifier of Task 1.4.3i (at most 51,200 bytes in all), then imports the v2 sheets of a project that holds one; it reads no classic sheet and no model body, writes nothing to the sidecar and leaves a classic-only project in the bundle untouched; a mixed project found this way shows its v2 sheets only until the owner turns the setting On; projects beyond a bound wait for the next open. The default build therefore delivers new-editor sketches in OPFS, v2 sheets synced from desktop and unchanged classic sketches, and not the bundle, exports or `preview.svg` of classic sketches; the keep or drop rule and the post-spike demand check count exactly that demand (block `web-value`). Tests: `ClassicWebFileSidePolicyTest.classicSheetOfAMixedProject_should_StayDatabaseOnlyUnderOff`, `SyncedV2ProjectDiscoveryTest` (`syncedV2ProjectNotInDatabase_should_BeImportedUnderOff`, `classicOnlySyncedProject_should_BeLeftUntouched`, `mixedProject_should_ImportV2SheetsOnly`, `scan_should_StopAtTheProjectAndTimeBounds_And_ResumeAtTheNextOpen`, `scan_should_ReadOnlyTheListingAnd256BytesOfOneSheet_And_WriteNothing`) and `WasmClassicReconcileTest.settingOffAndSyncedV2Project_should_OpenEditable`. Owner: Tyler.
  - D-154 The classic file side gate is installed before anything can make wasm storage available (Tasks 1.4.7o, 1.4.7j, 1.4.7n; engineering E1, data-loss class; supersedes the placement of the gate in D-147): Task 1.4.7j replaces the interim override of Task 1.4.3m and so turns `supportsTempRenameWrites`, `supportsAtomicReplace` and `storageStatus` on for wasm, and because store availability is the file system's capability, from that task on every classic project of an Available browser would run the classic file side (write-through, the `SidecarReconciler` pull with `replaceProjectContents`, `importNew`) in waves W11 and W12, ungated, until the gate of Task 1.4.7n (W13). The gate (the pure per-sheet `ClassicWebFileSidePolicy`, its hook in `GraphContentViewModelSetup.kt` and the setting accessor, default `Off`, closed) is now its own Task 1.4.7o (W9, after Story 1.4.3, before Task 1.4.7j); Task 1.4.7n keeps the discovery scan, the owner switch and `WasmClassicReconcileTest`. The block `plan-availability-switch` lists every task that can make the wasm storage available with its gate task, and `check-plan.py` fails when the gate is not an ancestor in an earlier wave. Owner: Tyler.
  - D-155 The durability cue: four forms, the windows without `HintStatus` computed once, a cue for the temporary graph and the chip-row fit (UX-153, UX-151, UX-150; ux.md 14.8 blocks `hint-cue`, `cue-forms`, `save-status-names`; supersedes the two-form cue of D-151): "Private: may clear." as a chip needs 11 dp of padding plus 19 characters x 7 dp x 2.0 = 277 dp, but the chip row of the small phone at font scale 2.0 is 196 dp (the overlay is 244 dp, less the 44 dp mic button and one gap), so a cue the plan calls persistent would have scrolled out of its row. Each cue now has four forms (full, mid, compact, chip: "Private", "Temporary") and the chip shows the longest form that fits the chip row of its window, computed from the chrome table for EVERY window without a `HintStatus` row: the union of the Rows-collapsed column (the small phone at 2.0) and the `permanent` section (the landscape phones and the 400% zoom windows, which D-151 had left out), listed once by the computed block. A temporary graph had no persistent cue (its "Saved for this session only" is a tooltip, invisible on touch, and the save status is glyph-only from font scale 1.5), so it gets the same mechanism with its own forms. The action chip row is no longer "only while something is active": the cue chip is its one persistent, non-dismissible occupant, and the overlay height of the chrome table already reserves the row. The accessible name of "Saving..." now begins with its visible label (WCAG 2.5.3), and the `StillSaving` announcement second is defined once (UX-150 no longer says "after 1 s of visibility"). Owner: Tyler.

  - D-105 Header-first key order for the v2 sheet (ADR-001 item 5; Tasks 1.1.2a, 1.1.5a, 1.1.5c and 1.4.3i; the `NewSketchProject` predicate reads it; engineering E1): the canonical emitter writes `schema` then `sheetUuid` first and sorts only the rest, because a fully sorted emission would put `schema` after `constraints`, `elements`, `extras` and `layers`, beyond the 256-byte prefix of any real sheet, and the header probe could never say V2; a v2 file begins `{"schema":2,"sheetUuid":"` and a v1 file (pretty-printed by `SidecarCodec`) begins `{\n  "schema": 1,`; `classifyHeader` requires `schema` as the first key within `SCHEMA_WINDOW` = 64 bytes; the alternative, a tolerant scan of the whole prefix, was rejected because it accepts a `"schema"` key nested inside an element. `check-plan.py` computes the offsets. Owner: Tyler.
  - D-106 Model-only read and a null-returning prefix default for the `NewSketchProject` predicate (Tasks 1.4.3g, 1.4.3h and 1.4.3i; engineering E2, E4): `ProjectSidecarStore.readModel(slug)` reads ONE file, `project-model.json` (the code file name; the interface had no such read and `readLocked` reads every sheet in full), and `isAvailable()` reports the platform capability; `FileSystem.readFilePrefix` defaults to a truncated `readFileBytes` with `UnsupportedOperationException` mapped to null, because the default `readFileBytes` throws and a bare delegation would break every fake, iOS and wasmJs; the JVM override lives on `JvmFileSystemBase` (protected `validatePath`) and Android overrides it directly. Owner: Tyler.

## Dependency Visualization

```
Phase 0 spikes ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 0.1.1 old-reader gate ─┐ (0.1.1b store change gates 1.1.3, 1.4.2)                                                │
 0.1.2 solver spike ────┼────────────────────────────────► E4.1 solver    │
 0.1.3 determinism ─────┼──► 1.1.2 canon doubles, 2.1.2 text, 5.2.2 PNG   │
 0.1.4 input spike ─────┼──► E3.1 input                                   │
 0.1.5 fixtures/goldens ┼──► 2.1.4 freehand, 2.2.x rough, E5.1 import     │
 0.1.6 scene benchmark ─┴──► 1.2.1 scene, 1.3.3 index                     │
                                                                           │
Phase 1 (a DAG, not a chain; the old "serial spine" 1.2.1 ► 1.2.2 ► 1.2.3 ► 1.2.4 was wrong):
                        1.1.1 ► (1.1.2 is independent of it)
                        1.2.4a SceneLimits (FIRST in Epic 1.2) ► 1.2.1 ► { 1.2.2 ∥ 1.2.3 }  and  1.2.1 ► 1.3.1 ► { 1.3.2 ∥ 1.3.3 }
                        1.4.1 schema_version ► 1.1.3 bundle dispatch ► 1.1.4 technical data
                        1.2.4a + 1.2.1 + 1.2.3 + 1.1.2 ► 1.1.5 SheetCodecV2 ► 1.4.5 fusion + preview wiring (also needs 1.1.3, 1.1.4, 1.2.2, 1.4.1)
                        3.6.4a diagnostics model+port (W3, reads `SceneLimits`) ► 1.4.4 autosave (needs 1.1.3, 1.1.5) ► W7 headless slice
                        0.1.8 OPFS spike + 1.4.3g prefix read ► 1.4.7a verdict, 1.4.7e primitives ► 1.4.7f write queue ► 1.4.7b byte API ► 1.4.7g temp and rename ► 1.4.7j preload fact (also 1.4.7h, 1.4.7i) ► 1.4.7c durability test ► 1.4.7d web browser pass (also 1.4.7k, 1.4.7l, 6.2.4g, 5.1.5)
                        ► 1.4.2 conversion (needs OD-1; runs only on the explicit Upgrade action) ► 1.4.3 router/flag ► 1.4.6 shell UI (also 1.4.4, 3.6.4b, 3.6.7)
                        ► rendered slice (after 2.1.3) ► A1 GO/NO-GO GATE 6.2.4e (W10) ► gated UI stories (4.2.2, 4.2.3, 4.2.5, 4.2.6b, 4.2.7b, 4.2.7d, 5.1.5, 5.2.3, flag release)
Wave numbers (W1..W14) are defined once, in "Effort estimate > Critical path"; every story sits in the wave after its last dependency.
Phase 2-6: see the story-level edges below (phase labels are only a reading order).
```

**Machine-readable edge list (authoritative)**. The prose table above is the human reading; this block is what the script `check-plan.py` in validation.md reads to compute every wave (wave = 1 + the maximum wave of the node's dependencies; `OWNER0` is the batched owner session, wave 0). A node is a story id, or a task id when that task is scheduled apart from its story. Tasks without their own node run in their story's node. Editing an edge means editing this block and re-running the script; the declared waves in "Effort estimate > Critical path" must equal the computed ones.

**Pre-check hold (P3; requirements.md "Sunk cost before the A1 gate", Task 0.1.7c).** The pre-check is performed INSIDE the batched OWNER0 owner session, before any agent starts (D-107: it asks the owner about their next 5 diagrams and needs nothing from engineering), so it is wave 0 and costs no agent work. In the edge block it is the line `0.1.7c: @OWNER0`, every W1 node lists it, and `check-plan.py` fails on any node with no path from it, so a FAIL reaches all 161 other nodes, not only a hand-named set. When the pre-check FAILS (0 or 1 of 5 diagrams in-app) nothing starts until the owner records a choice. The KEPT line names the pure engines that continue under "stop at the pure engines"; the held set is every Epic 2 and Epic 3 node outside the dependency closure of KEPT, except the NONUI diagnostics model and chrome-fixture tooling; each ROOT of the held set (a held node with no held predecessor) also lists node 0.1.7c directly, kept as the structural record of which nodes a FAIL holds, and `check-plan.py` fails if the HELD line, the closure or any root edge is wrong. Node 0.1.7c is complete only when `baselines.md` holds the PASS, MARGINAL or FAIL result and, on FAIL, the owner's recorded choice; if the choice is "stop entirely" every node is recorded `Skipped(pre-check)` and nothing is built; if the choice is "stop at the pure engines" every node outside the dependency closure of KEPT and outside the NONUI tooling is recorded `Skipped(pre-check)`, the other W1 nodes included, and the project ends at KEPT with nothing user-visible shipped. The MERGES line names the kept nodes that change shipped code (the forward-only database migration, the store `backup/` change and the bundle dispatch): under the engine stop they merge to `main` and stay unreachable behind `ConversionReady = false` and mode `Off`, and `check-plan.py` fails if one of them is not inside the kept closure (D-109). The HELD line covers Epics 2 and 3 only, so the stop set is wider than it: it also holds the solver runner (4.1.4, needs the binding engine 3.3.2), the embed preview dispatch (6.1.1, needs the painter 2.1.3), the reader-only release (6.2.5e, needs 6.1.1 and 2.1.3), the A1 gate (6.2.4e) and the flag release (6.2.5c); the `STOPNAMED` line below names those and `check-plan.py` fails unless the computed stop set contains each. If the choice is "continue, adoption is not the goal" the hold is released and nothing else changes: the A1 gate (Task 6.2.4e) still runs as written, including PIVOT on "would not continue" and its fourth outcome "unmeasured under the cap" (precedence in requirements.md "Combined outcomes": the hold is read first and never overrides the gate; the gate never re-imposes the hold).

<!-- plan-hold:start -->
```text
KEPT: 4.1.1 4.1.2 4.1.3 5.1.1 5.1.2 5.1.3 5.1.4 5.2.1 5.2.2 6.1.2
NOTKEPT: 0.1.2d 0.1.3d
MERGES: 0.1.1b 1.1.3 1.4.1
NONUI: 3.6.4a 3.6.4c 3.6.4d 3.6.4e 3.6.4f
```

# Phase 0: Spikes and gates (riskiest first)

### Epic 0.1: Verify the unverified before building on it
**Goal**: Convert every research "UNVERIFIED/INFERRED" that can sink the plan into a measured result or a golden test.

#### Story 0.1.1: Old reader fails closed on schema 2
**As a** maintainer, **I want** a golden proof that today's reader refuses a higher schema, **so that** v2 sheets can never be re-saved as v1.
**Verified already (code read)**: `SidecarClassifier.classifyJson` maps `schema > 1` to `Corrupt(NewerSchema)` (`CM/projectbridge/SidecarFileState.kt:41`); `ProjectSidecarStore.readLocked` returns `Corrupt` for the whole project on the first bad file (`ProjectSidecarStore.kt:89-93`). This story pins that behaviour in tests and checks the rest of the path.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- A bundle whose model REFERENCES a `schema:3` sheet is `SidecarRead.Corrupt(NewerSchema, <sheet path>)` and nothing is written. (Schema 3 is the fixture because it is newer than both today's limit of 1 and the post-Story-1.1.1 sheet limit of 2, so this gate stays valid after the per-kind split; "old build" behaviour for a schema:2 sheet is simulated in Task 1.1.1c with the previous max.)
  - *Given* a `ProjectBundle` fixture with sheet `s1` (schema 1) and sheet `s3` (`{"schema":3,"sheetUuid":"s3","scene":{}}`, referenced by the model at the default path `sheets/s3.json`), *When* `ProjectSidecarStore.read("kitchen")` runs, *Then* the result is `SidecarRead.Corrupt(CorruptReason.NewerSchema, ".../sheets/s3.json")` and file bytes of `s1.json`, `s3.json`, `project-model.json` are unchanged afterwards.
- A file in `backup/` is invisible to the reader.
  - *Given* `kitchen/backup/s1.v1.json` exists beside a valid bundle, *When* `read("kitchen")` runs, *Then* the result is `SidecarRead.Present` with exactly the model and `sheets/*.json` files.
- The store supports `backup/` and unreferenced sheet files (Task 0.1.1b).
  - *Given* `FileProjectSidecarStore` writing a bundle after `backup/s1.v1.json` was created through the store's new `writeBackup(slug, uuid, bytes)`, *When* the bundle is written and read back, *Then* `backup/` exists, `Present(files)` and `bundleHash` are identical to the same bundle without a backup, a later `writeLocked` does NOT delete `backup/*`, and an unreferenced `sheets/s1.v2.json` is never opened by the new reader (so even a garbage or `schema:3` unreferenced file is not a `Corrupt`) and is not part of the hash set; the generic `writeLocked` sweep deletes only unreferenced files whose bytes classify as schema 1 (an unreferenced schema-2, schema-3 or unparseable file is left in place; a staged `sheets/s1.v2.json` is removed only by the converter's cleanup or restore through `deleteV2Sheet`, Pre-mortem P1 #1), and a file in `conflicts/` is likewise invisible to `read`, the hash and the sweep.
- `backup/` and `conflicts/` have a stated growth, git-sync and ignore policy (`RetentionPolicy`; reliability gap, engineering lens item 12). Defaults, all owner-confirmable (D-6): (a) **`backup/` is synced by git** like the rest of the sidecar, because it is the only restore source on other devices; it holds at most one `<uuid>.v1.json` per converted sheet (the original, never overwritten, never auto-pruned) plus at most one `<uuid>.v2-before-restore.json` per restore (Story 6.2.6), so its growth is bounded by the number of converted sheets times the original sheet size; Settings > Sketches shows its total size and a "Delete backups" action with a confirmation (Task 6.2.6c); (b) **`conflicts/` is device-local**: `ensureIgnoreRule` also writes `assets/stelekit-sketch/**/conflicts/` to the graph `.gitignore` (the existing rule covers only `*.tmp`, `ProjectSidecarStore.kt:184`), the newest 10 copies per sheet are kept, older ones are pruned only after the sheet's saver has resolved the conflict (Reload or Keep mine) and never while an unresolved conflict entry exists; (c) a graph or sidecar directory that is git-ignored by the user keeps working (all of this is local files; nothing assumes a remote), and `ensureIgnoreRule` never rewrites an existing user ignore line.
  - *Given* 12 converted sheets and 25 conflict copies for one sheet, *When* the store prunes, *Then* `backup/` still holds exactly 12 originals, `conflicts/` holds the newest 10 for that sheet, an unresolved conflict's copy is never pruned, `git check-ignore` classifies `conflicts/*` as ignored and `backup/*` as not ignored, and `Present(files)` and `bundleHash` are unchanged by any of it.
**Files**: `BT/projectbridge/SidecarOldReaderSchemaGateTest.kt` (new), `BT/projectbridge/SidecarBackupAndStagingTest.kt` (new), `CM/projectbridge/ProjectSidecarStore.kt`, `kmp/src/businessTest/resources/sketch/old-reader/*.json` (fixtures)

##### Task 0.1.1a: Fixtures and gate test (S)
- Build the fixtures; assert Corrupt(NewerSchema) and byte-for-byte unchanged files; assert `backup/` ignored; register in `AllBusinessTests`.
- Files: the two above, `AllBusinessTests.kt`
##### Task 0.1.1c: `RetentionPolicy` for `backup/` and `conflicts/` (prune rule, `conflicts/` ignore rule, size accounting API) with `BT/projectbridge/SidecarRetentionPolicyTest.kt` (S)
##### Task 0.1.1b: Store change: `backup/`, unreferenced-sheet handling, atomic model write (S)
- This is a code change, not a note. In `FileProjectSidecarStore`: (1) `writeBackup` creates `<slug>/backup/` and writes `<uuid>.v1.json` (write-temp-then-rename, then re-read and compare sha before returning); (2) `readLocked` ignores `backup/` entirely; (3) REORDER `readLocked` (today, in `ProjectSidecarStore.kt` L78-96 at `54f059f351`, it lists every `sheets/*.json` at L81 and classifies each in the loop at L86-94 before the model's references are known): read and classify the model FIRST, decode its `sheetRef`s, then read ONLY the sheet files the model references (`sheetRef.file`, default `sheets/<uuid>.json` per `SheetPaths`, Story 1.1.3); a `sheets/*.json` file that no `sheetRef` references is `Unreferenced`: never opened, never classified, excluded from `files` and `bundleHash` (the OLD reader still scans every `sheets/*.json` and fails closed, which is the intended safe direction); (4) classification of each referenced file uses the per-kind limit (model 1, sheet 2) introduced by Task 1.1.1a, so the new reader accepts a referenced `schema:2` sheet and refuses `schema:3`; until 1.1.1a lands this task keeps the single limit; (5) `writeLocked` deletes unreferenced `sheets/*.json` after the model write succeeds ONLY when the file's bytes classify as schema 1 (a schema-2 file, including a staged `sheets/<uuid>.v2.json` from an interrupted conversion, is deleted only through the explicit `deleteV2Sheet(slug, uuid)` call used by the converter's cleanup and by restore, never by the generic sweep; an unparseable or newer-schema unreferenced file is left alone), and never touches `backup/` or `conflicts/`; `conflicts/` is created and written like `backup/` through a NEW method `writeSheetConflictCopy(slug, sheetUuid, bytes)` (path `<slug>/conflicts/<uuid>.v2.conflict-<hash8>.json`; same temp+rename+sha read-back) and is excluded from `read`, `Present(files)` and `bundleHash`. **Relation to the existing `writeConflict(slug, files)`** (read at `ProjectSidecarStore.kt:44,132-140`): that method is the reconciler's and stays UNCHANGED; it keeps the local project MODEL beside a sidecar the reconcile will not overwrite, takes a bundle map and writes only `project-model.conflict-<hash8>.json` at the slug root (not in `conflicts/`). The new method has a different purpose (a v2 SHEET scene saved by `SheetSaver`), a different signature (one sheet's bytes) and a different location, so there is no name or path collision; it reuses only the private `replace()` temp+rename helper and the store mutex. The two kinds are not merged: `project-model.conflict-*` files are outside `RetentionPolicy` pruning (which covers only `conflicts/`) and are never read as sheets; (6) the model file write is write-temp-then-atomic-rename so the model flip is a single atomic step; verify the existing implementation and fix it if it is not. Record the verified facts in ADR-001.
- Files: `CM/projectbridge/ProjectSidecarStore.kt`, `BT/projectbridge/SidecarBackupAndStagingTest.kt`, `ADR-001` (verified facts)

#### Story 0.1.2: Constraint solver spike (gates Epic 4.1)
**As a** maintainer, **I want** evidence that a pure-Kotlin LM solver meets the budget and robustness bar, **so that** ADR-003 can be Accepted.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Exit criteria from ADR-003 item 6 are measured and recorded.
  - *Given* a generated 100-constraint sketch of 40 `Line`s with Coincident/Horizontal/Vertical/Equal/Distance, *When* the spike solver runs on JVM and wasmJs, *Then* wall time is under 50 ms on JVM and under 150 ms on wasmJs (hard bars; 50 ms on wasmJs is an advisory target recorded, not gating; D-30) and max constraint residual is under 1e-9 of sketch scale (solver stop tolerance; the acceptance bar is 1e-6 scene units).
- Redundancy and conflict classification is correct.
  - *Given* `Line L1` with `Horizontal(L1)` and `Vertical(L1)` plus a duplicate `Horizontal(L1)`, *When* solved, *Then* the result is `Redundant([c3])` and DOF equals the analytic value; adding `Distance(L1)=10` and `Distance(L1)=12` yields `Conflict([c4,c5])`.
- No branch flip on drag.
  - *Given* a tangent line-circle pair and a 200-step scripted drag of the circle centre by 0.5 unit steps, *When* solving from the previous solution each step, *Then* no point moves more than 3x the pointer delta and the tangent side never changes.
**Precondition**: OD-3 (clean-room vs port) is answered BEFORE Task 0.1.2a starts, so no spike code is written under an unsettled license position.
**Files**: `CT/sketch/constraints/SolverSpikeTest.kt`, `SOLV/spike/*.kt` (throwaway, kept until Epic 4.1 replaces them), `benchmarks/budgets/sketch-engine-budgets.json`

##### Task 0.1.2a: Dense linear algebra + Jacobian rank (M)
- `DoubleArray` Cholesky/QR with column pivoting; rank and null-space size; fuzz for NaN. Clean-room note: no planegcs source open.
##### Task 0.1.2b: LM loop + 5 constraint types + oracle fixtures (M)
- Coincident, Horizontal, Vertical, Equal, Distance, Tangent(line-circle); capture planegcs/SolveSpace results on the generated cases once, offline, as JSON fixtures (oracle only, not linked).
##### Task 0.1.2c: wasmJs run + report (S)
- `:kmp:wasmJsBrowserTest` timing and the JVM-vs-wasm diff (target under 1e-6); write results into ADR-003 and flip its status.
**Abort/pivot thresholds (requirements, Abort and pivot criteria; decided per target, D-114)**: the spike records a `SolverTier` (`Full`, `GeometricOnly` or `Hidden`) for JVM and for wasmJs. The target-independent criteria (max residual under 1e-6 scene units, `Redundant`/`Conflict` classification matches the analytic result on the fixtures, no branch flip on the 200-step drag) select `GeometricOnly` on EVERY target when any is missed; the time criterion (100-constraint solve under 50 ms on JVM and under 150 ms on wasmJs, the single wasmJs figure everywhere; 50 ms there is advisory, D-30) decides the tier of ITS OWN target only: a wasmJs time miss makes wasmJs `GeometricOnly` (the geometric-only set is re-measured there and the target is `Hidden` if that misses too) and leaves JVM and Android on `Full`. If even the geometric-only set misses the residual bar on every target, Epic 4.1/4.2 are deferred and the owner is asked (requirements.md owns that decision). The fallback is accepted per target against the "fallback plate" scenario of requirements metric 3 (Story 4.1.3), the bracket plate on a `Full` target; ADR-003 carries the 1e-6 residual bar in its spike exit criteria.
**Fallback if the spike fails (pre-mortem P2 #5; per target, D-114)**: on every target whose `SolverTier` is `GeometricOnly` the feature ships GEOMETRIC-ONLY in its first release: coincident, horizontal/vertical, equal and distance, with `NoConvergence` surfaced to the user (never a silent best effort); tangent, symmetric, angle, Fixed, Radius and Parallel stay hidden (the set of requirements.md metric 3). Independently of the spike result, Epic 4.2 exposes only the constraint kinds that have a passing randomised-sketch property test (Story 4.1.3); unproven kinds are not offered in the Constrain flyout.
##### Task 0.1.2d: Job 2 demand check (A6) (XS); owner Tyler
- After Task 0.1.2c, the owner lists the next 3 parts or layouts they would dimension and marks which they would do in-app rather than in FreeCAD or on paper, and records each part's platform (`desktop`, `android`, `web` with the browser, or `ios`) and project kind (`new`, `existing-classic`, `existing-classic-technical` or `existing-v2`; requirements.md "Reachable demand": only parts reachable by kind and by platform count as in-app, and a part with technical data in an existing classic project is unreachable unless the owner records a plan to remove or move it); the result and the planner's recommendation (proceed / adjust to geometric-only / defer Epic 4.2 UI) are recorded in `implementation/a6-job2-demand.md`. Outcome names and the threshold are owned by requirements.md "A6 go/no-go" (PROCEED at 1 or more of 3 in-app, ADJUST (at least 1 of 3 in-app AND at least 1 of the 14 Constrain flyout entries crossed out, or the geometric-only fallback) narrows to the constraint kinds the owner needs, DEFER at 0 of 3: park, never delete, the six constraint-UI items and Task 6.2.4f (1.18M CU, 3.5% of the total); Epic 4.1 (no edge from this task reaches it) and 4.2.1/4.2.4/4.2.6a/4.2.7a/4.2.7c are built in every outcome; 4.2.7c waits for this record only because ADJUST narrows the kinds it may suggest, and its S cost is outside the 1.18M CU). Priority of Job 1 over Job 2 is recorded in requirements.md "Priority and roadmap fit".

#### Story 0.1.3: Cross-target determinism spike
**As a** maintainer, **I want** measured JVM/Android/wasmJs differences for doubles, canonical JSON, text metrics and offscreen PNG, **so that** the "identical rendering" claim is evidence-based.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Canonical double formatting is byte-identical across targets.
  - *Given* the values `[0.1, 1e-5, 123456789.125, -0.0, 4.0]`, *When* serialised by the commonMain routine on JVM and wasmJs, *Then* the byte strings are equal and `4.0` serialises as `4`.
- Text metrics tolerance recorded.
  - *Given* the bundled font and the strings "Auth Service", "データ", "line one\nline two" at 20 px, *When* measured on JVM and wasmJs, *Then* widths differ by at most the tolerance written into the plan (target 1 px) and line breaks are identical for a 120 px wrap width.
- Offscreen `ImageBitmap` draw works on wasmJs.
  - *Given* a rect + text draw list, *When* drawn into an offscreen `ImageBitmap` on wasmJs, *Then* a non-blank 200x100 bitmap is produced (or the result is "not possible", which switches Story 5.2.2 on wasm to a Canvas-element seam).
- CJK fallback policy is decided by measurement.
  - *Given* "データ" and "你好" with the candidate bundled fonts (OD-4), *When* measured on JVM, Android and wasmJs, *Then* the result is one of: (a) a bundled CJK subset font gives widths within 1 px on all targets, or (b) the policy "CJK text uses the platform fallback font; its metrics are persisted per element, excluded from cross-target goldens, and wrap points for CJK are not asserted across targets" is written into ADR-004.
- Text-layout pivot is explicit (requirements, Abort and pivot criteria).
  - *Given* the Latin fixtures, *When* widths differ by more than 1 px between JVM and wasmJs or any line break differs at the 120 px wrap width, *Then* the plan switches to "persisted metrics authoritative and cross-target pixel goldens exclude text" (metric 4 judged on shapes and strokes) and, if breaks still differ with persisted metrics, text is pixel-golden on JVM only; the outcome is written into ADR-004.
- Android pixel-render feasibility.
  - *Given* the rect + text draw list, *When* drawn under Robolectric (native graphics) and compared to the JVM render, *Then* the diff is within a recorded tolerance, or the result is "Robolectric not reliable for Compose text and paths", which switches Android pixel parity to draw-list equality plus the on-device owner check (Story 6.2.4c) and removes Robolectric from Task 2.1.3c; requirements metric 4 then reports the Android column as "owner-checked", never "CI-verified".
**Files**: `CT/sketch/v2/DeterminismSpikeTest.kt`, `JT/...` and wasm test counterpart, `AT/sketch/v2/AndroidPixelSpikeTest.kt`
##### Task 0.1.3a: Shortest-round-trip double formatter prototype + cross-target test (S)
##### Task 0.1.3b: Text metrics comparison with the bundled font, plus CJK fallback policy decision (S)
##### Task 0.1.3c: Offscreen ImageBitmap + PNG encode feasibility on wasmJs and iOS compile (S)
##### Task 0.1.3d: Float drift check for the solver fixtures (cross-link 0.1.2c) (XS)
##### Task 0.1.3e: Android pixel-render feasibility under Robolectric vs JVM (S)

#### Story 0.1.4: Input spike (pointer kinds, pressure, touch-action, iOS compile)
**As a** maintainer, **I want** to know what Compose pointer input exposes per target, **so that** the stylus/touch split and freehand pressure design match reality.
**Verified so far**: no `PointerType` use under `CM/ui/sketch` (grep, this session); one-finger drag currently pans (`SketchGestures.kt:38-78` per research).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- A matrix is written into `project_plans/excalidraw-sketch-engine/research/input-spike.md` (a dedicated, discoverable note; ADR-007 links to it): per target (Desktop, Android, wasmJs, iOS compile) whether `PointerType`, pressure and hover are available from Compose core, whether `compose-stylus` is needed, and whether the stylus eraser-end tool type and the barrel-button state are exposed (Story 3.1.2, UX-101).
  - *Given* a test canvas logging `PointerInputChange` fields, *When* driven by mouse (desktop), Robolectric `MotionEvent` stylus (`TOOL_TYPE_STYLUS`, pressure 0.6) and a wasm `pointerType=pen` event, *Then* the log shows `type=Stylus pressure=0.6` on Android and states the wasm value (0.6 or "unavailable").
- The wasm canvas host stops browser gestures.
  - *Given* the wasm canvas host element, *When* two-finger pinch is dispatched, *Then* the page does not scroll (`touch-action: none` verified) and both pointers reach the handler.
- System-gesture exclusion, edge deferral and palm behaviour are measured, not assumed (UX-102; D-18).
  - *Given* the Pixel_Fold AVD, a touch-capable desktop and the wasm host, *When* a one-finger drag starts inside the 44 dp strip that begins at the system gesture inset (`WindowInsets.systemGestures`; UX-121 geometry: 24 dp visible band plus 20 dp hit-only), once INSIDE and once OUTSIDE a 200 dp vertical protected span, and a drag starts in the inset itself, *Then* `research/input-spike.md` records per target whether the app received the drag in each of the three places: Android `View.setSystemGestureExclusionRects` honoured or not (with the platform's per-edge cap on excluded height, documented at 200 dp since API 29 and re-verified here, not assumed); iOS `preferredScreenEdgesDeferringSystemGestures` compile-only (device behaviour UNVERIFIED); web `overscroll-behavior: none` plus `touch-action: none` stopping back-swipe on Chrome Android (a Safari iOS edge swipe cannot be blocked: recorded as such); and what `TOOL_TYPE_PALM` and `ACTION_CANCEL` report on a touch laptop or tablet. The result marks each target class Supported or NOT Supported (Supported only when a real drag that started in the protected span reached the app on every attempt of at least 20); that mark chooses the default `Edge pan side` (Both where Supported, OFF everywhere else, so a negative or missing result ships with the strip off, not broken) and says whether the cancel-detection backstop of Task 3.1.2g is needed on each Supported target. The granted span height is recorded (200 dp expected, re-verified).
**Files**: `JT/sketch/v2/InputSpikeTest.kt` (`// jvmTest-reason: desktop-only`), `AT/sketch/v2/InputSpikeAndroidTest.kt`, wasm host file under `kmp/src/wasmJsMain`, `project_plans/excalidraw-sketch-engine/research/input-spike.md` (stub with the matrix skeleton exists; every cell reads UNMEASURED until the spike fills it)

#### Story 0.1.5: Fixtures, goldens and license re-check
**As a** maintainer, **I want** captured upstream fixtures and license facts, **so that** ports and the importer have oracles.
**Blockers named (not hidden)**: capturing real exports needs a browser and network; running the JS originals needs Node and network (npm). Fixtures are therefore HAND-AUTHORED from the `types.ts` element definitions (no network needed beyond reading the upstream source); real excalidraw.com exports supplied by Tyler are an optional, additive fixture set (owner: Tyler, non-blocking). Goldens for freehand/rough need Node + network once: if the executing agent has neither, Stories 2.1.4 and 2.2.1 start with self-consistency/property tests and the golden assertions are added when Tyler or a networked run supplies the JSON (owner: Tyler; blocks only the golden ACs, not the stories).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Excalidraw sample files exist for the importer.
  - *Given* five hand-authored scenes written from `types.ts` (shapes+text, bound arrows, frame+group, image, freedraw) plus one legacy `{focus,gap}` file, *When* stored under `kmp/src/commonTest/resources/excalidraw/`, *Then* each parses as JSON and the element types in `types.ts` (including `stickynote`/`magicframe` presence) are checked against the repo source and recorded.
- perfect-freehand and rough.js golden outputs exist.
  - *Given* a 30-point input with pressures and seed 1337, *When* the JS originals run once, *Then* the outline polygon / rough op list is saved as JSON fixtures; both upstream LICENSE texts are recorded as MIT or the plan is escalated.
- Real exports and the baseline pin exist (requirements metric 7 and "Excalidraw feature baseline"; D-22, D-24; U8).
  - *Given* Task 0.1.5d, *When* it completes, *Then* `kmp/src/commonTest/resources/excalidraw/real/` holds at least 5 owner-supplied real excalidraw.com exports (at least one with an image, one with bound arrows, one with a frame), `research/features.md` records the Excalidraw release tag and date that `types.ts` and the shortcuts dialog were read from (the pin of the baseline) together with the result of the U8 check (shortcuts and tool-lock behaviour compared with the live dialog), and if fewer than 5 exports exist by the start of Story 5.1.2 (W7) `FixtureRoundTripTest` prints `hand-authored fixtures only, UNMEASURED against real exports`.
**Files**: `kmp/src/commonTest/resources/excalidraw/*.excalidraw`, `.../freehand-golden.json`, `.../rough-golden.json`, `NOTICE`

#### Story 0.1.7: Baselines and the A1 pre-check (measured before the bars that cite them)
**As a** maintainer, **I want** the reference numbers that requirements metrics 2(b), 8 and the performance SLO compare against to be measured and recorded, **so that** no bar is judged against an assumed baseline.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- The owner's excalidraw.com time exists (metric 2b; Task 0.1.7a).
  - *Given* the 15-element diagram of requirements metric 2 (9 labelled boxes, 4 bound arrows, 1 frame, 1 group) as a one-page drawing sheet, *When* the owner draws it in excalidraw.com on desktop and on the phone, first attempt, 3 runs per device, *Then* the median per device is written into `implementation/baselines.md` with the date, device and browser; until that file has the numbers, metric 2(b) is reported "unmeasured", not "met".
- The performance reference exists (Task 0.1.7b).

- The A1 pre-check and the 50% bar are justified (Task 0.1.7c).
  - *Given* the owner's list of the next 5 diagrams they expect to need, *When* they mark which would be authored in-app and record each item's platform (the SteleKit platform it WOULD be authored on, also for an "elsewhere" item: `desktop`, `android`, `web` with its browser families `chromium`, `firefox` or `safari`, or `ios`) and project kind (`new`, `existing-classic`, `existing-classic-technical` or `existing-v2`), *Then* the list, the platforms, the kinds and the count are written into `baselines.md` (only reachable items count as in-app, so an `existing-classic-technical` item counts only with a recorded plan to remove or move its technical data, an `ios` item never counts, and a `web` item counts provisionally until the Spike 0.1.8 verdict of its browser exists; a later PIVOT that caps the row triggers one recount recorded beside the original; requirements.md "Reachable demand"), together with the owner's CURRENT diagram frequency (new diagrams per month, a number, from the owner's own history); the 50% adoption bar of metric 8 is restated next to it (advisory, INFERRED, recalibrated if the owner's own list says otherwise), the metric-8 read window is computed from the frequency (8 weeks, extended until at least 5 new diagrams are expected, never beyond 26 weeks; requirements metric 8, D-46), and the A1 gate (Task 6.2.4e) will draw one diagram from this list; the same record carries the Option W inputs (D-138): the count `webItems` (Chromium-family or Firefox web items) and, when it is 0, the line `web-intent: yes -- <named use>, <family>` or `web-intent: no`, which `check-plan.py` cannot read but the keep or drop rule of requirements.md applies as written.
  - Pass threshold (D-27): PASS when at least 3 of the 5 are marked in-app (reachable ones only; 60%, the sanity check of the 50% bar); MARGINAL when exactly 2 of 5 (40%): proceed, record the reason, and the A1 gate decides; FAIL when 0 or 1 of 5 (recorded by the owner as "no demand" or "structural", D-115): the held set of "Pre-check hold" (the Epic 2 and 3 UI stories, named and script-checked there) does not start until the owner records one of "stop entirely" (no agent work is started (0.00M CU), because the pre-check is read before W1), "stop at the pure engines" (Stories 4.1.1-4.1.3, 5.1.1-5.1.4, 5.2.1, 5.2.2 and 6.1.2 and everything they depend on; only the KEPT closure is built (33 nodes: 28 outside W1, 6185k raw tokens, 9.15M CU, and 5 W1 spikes it needs, 795k raw tokens, 1.18M CU; 10.33M CU in all, 31.1% of the total); the closure of the plan-hold KEPT line less NOTKEPT) or "continue, adoption is not the goal" in `baselines.md`; the hold is enforced by an edge from node 0.1.7c to every W1 node and every root of the held set, so no story can be scheduled before the answer is recorded; the choice between the two stops follows the rule of requirements.md "Choosing between the two stops" (D-109). A stop records every node outside the kept closure `Skipped(pre-check)` (including Stories 4.1.4 and 6.1.1 and Tasks 6.2.5e, 6.2.4e and 6.2.5c) and ships nothing; "continue" only releases the hold and leaves the A1 gate as written. No agent work is sunk when this check is read (it is read inside the OWNER0 session before W1); the W1 cost, 2.23M CU of the 33.20M CU raw estimate, is what a PASS or MARGINAL result starts (requirements.md "Sunk cost before the A1 gate").
- The regression baseline for metric 6 is captured BEFORE any engine change (Task 0.1.7d).
  - *Given* the pre-change commit (the standing sketch PRs merged, no `V2` code present), *When* `scripts/sketch-baseline/record-sketch-baseline.sh` runs, *Then* it writes `project_plans/excalidraw-sketch-engine/implementation/regression-baseline.txt` whose FIRST line is `baseline-sha: <40-hex commit>` (the commit the engine branch forks from on `main`, `git merge-base origin/main HEAD`, or an explicit `--sha`; the script REFUSES a SHA that is not an ancestor of `origin/main`, because a SHA that exists only on the engine branch disappears when that branch is squash-merged, and re-derivation at the end would then fail), and the content is derived ONLY from `git ls-tree -r <sha>` and `git show <sha>:<path> | sha256sum` at that SHA (never from the working tree, so nothing added after the SHA can enter the baseline), containing the sorted list of every existing sketch test class (paths under `CT`, `BT`, `JT`, `AT` whose name or package matches `sketch`/`Sketch`, excluding `sketch/v2`), the sorted list of every sketch golden fixture file (with its SHA-256), and the two counts, the file is committed in W1, and re-running the script with the recorded SHA from any later checkout reproduces it byte for byte.

#### Story 0.1.8: OPFS atomic-write spike per browser (Option W; gates Story 1.4.7; D-104, D-112)
**As a** maintainer, **I want** the OPFS behaviours that Option W relies on measured in Chrome, Firefox and Safari, **so that** each browser's `OpfsStorageVerdict` is decided by evidence and a browser that cannot do it keeps the classic-only state instead of shipping a broken editor.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- The criteria of requirements.md "Web first release: what works" (four gating, a to d, and the persistence answer e, recorded and never gating) are measured per browser and recorded in `research/opfs-spike.md`; every browser-API fact the plan states stays INFERRED / UNVERIFIED until that file has the browser's row.
  - *Given* a 2 MB byte array and a temp-then-replace sequence, *When* `createWritable()` is opened, written and then (a) closed or (b) aborted, *Then* a reader sees the old bytes until the close, the new bytes after it and the old bytes after an abort; *Given* `FileSystemFileHandle.move()` over an existing file, *Then* the destination holds the new bytes and the temp name is gone, or the harness records `move` unsupported and runs the copy-with-verify path instead; *Given* a stored sheet, *Then* `file.slice(0, 256).arrayBuffer()` returns exactly 256 bytes; *Given* `navigator.storage.persist()` called from a click handler, *Then* the answer (granted, denied, absent) and whether the browser showed a prompt are recorded (they inform the warning of UX-143 and never gate the verdict); *Given* a stored sheet and `getFile()`, *Then* whether `lastModified` is available is recorded.
- A per-browser verdict and the pivot rule. *Given* a browser fails any of the four gating criteria, *Then* its row says `Unavailable` with the failing criterion and that browser keeps the classic-only state of requirements.md; *Given* neither Chrome nor Firefox passes, *Then* Option W is abandoned as a whole: Story 1.4.7 is recorded `Skipped(opfs-spike)` and the parked set `OPFSSPIKE` applies (D-127), and the owner re-records D-104; *Given* Chrome or Firefox passes but no browser family recorded by the pre-check does, *Then* the same abandon applies (D-139), unless the owner records that web is wanted without a counted item.
- The post-spike re-read (Task 0.1.8c; D-148). *Given* the recorded verdicts of Task 0.1.8b and the pre-check list of Task 0.1.7c, *When* the owner recounts the list by the `post-spike-recount` block of requirements.md at the end of W1, *Then* the gate is DECIDE exactly when the recount is FAIL while the recorded pre-check result was not (the owner then records `continue` or `stop`; `stop` uses the existing pre-check stop rows), otherwise the owner records `continue` in one line; no W2 node starts before the record exists (every W2 node has an edge from Task 0.1.8c, zero agent work).
**Files**: `kmp/src/wasmJsTest/kotlin/dev/stapler/stelekit/platform/OpfsAtomicWriteSpikeTest.kt` (a throwaway probe harness, replaced by `OpfsAtomicReplaceTest` in Story 1.4.7), `project_plans/excalidraw-sketch-engine/research/opfs-spike.md`

##### Task 0.1.8b: Owner runs the same harness in Firefox and Safari (desktop Safari, and iOS Safari for the web build there) and records each browser's row and verdict, then applies the demand check of D-139 (Chrome or Firefox Available but no counted family Available abandons Option W); Story 1.4.7 reads them (XS); owner; wave W1 wall-clock blocker
