##### Task 1.4.6k: The boot-refusal screen of the web build (UX-152, D-149): `BootRefusal` replaces the plain `<p>` of `showBootError` in `#loading` when `GraphLockedElsewhereException` aborts the boot, with a heading, an alert message as the one announcement (no focus move), [Reload] as the first tab stop with a visible focus ring, [Open temporarily] as the second, rem sizing, a retry that reloads when the lock frees or the `message-no-retry` variant where `navigator.locks.request` with a signal does not exist (the figures of the `boot-refusal` block of ux.md 14.9, D-156), with `BootRefusalTest` (S); after Task 1.4.6j

#### Story 1.4.7: Web file storage through OPFS atomic replace (Option W; D-104 owner decision, D-112, D-119 to D-145; needs 0.1.8, 1.4.3g, 1.4.3k, 1.4.3m)
**As a** web user, **I want** new-editor sketches to be saved, reloaded and replaced atomically in the browser and to be told truthfully when a save has reached browser storage, **so that** the Web first release has the new editor in every browser that can do it safely.
**Code facts this story is written against (read 2026-11-15; every browser-API fact stays INFERRED / UNVERIFIED until Spike 0.1.8 has a row for that browser)**: `AtomicFileWriter.write` writes the temp with `fs.writeFileBytes` (`commonMain/.../AtomicFileWriter.kt:65`), swaps with `fs.replaceFileAtomically` (`commonMain/.../AtomicFileWriter.kt:80`) and checks with `fs.readFileBytes` (`commonMain/.../AtomicFileWriter.kt:83`), all synchronous `Boolean`s, while the wasm file system updates its in-memory cache first and persists through a fire-and-forget `scope.async` that nobody awaits (`wasmJsMain/.../PlatformFileSystem.kt:564`); a synchronous function cannot await `createWritable()`, `move()` or a read-back, and a browser has no `fsync`, so a returned `true` means "accepted into the cache and queued", never "durable". `opfsWriteInFlight` holds ONE `Deferred` per path and each new write overwrites it (`wasmJsMain/.../PlatformFileSystem.kt:62`), so only the latest write of a path can be awaited and nothing orders a replace after the temp write. The `pagehide` hook only logs how many writes are in flight (`wasmJsMain/.../PlatformFileSystem.kt:194`). `writeFileBytes` records the path dirty and calls `scheduleHostWriteThrough` (`wasmJsMain/.../PlatformFileSystem.kt:574`); `renameFile` (`wasmJsMain/.../PlatformFileSystem.kt:610-618`) records nothing, ignores `bytesCache` and calls the host rename with a String; `getLastModifiedTime` (`wasmJsMain/.../PlatformFileSystem.kt:859`) reads only the host-folder map, so it is null for an OPFS-only file, and `StaleTempSweeper` skips a file whose `getLastModifiedTime` is null (`commonMain/.../StaleTempSweeper.kt:23`). `preload` swallows every OPFS failure and carries on with an empty cache (`wasmJsMain/.../PlatformFileSystem.kt:233`). The only cross-tab primitive is `WebLock.tryAcquireLeader` (`wasmJsMain/.../WebLock.kt:184`).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Per-browser verdict (pure; E7, D-125). *Given* the five probe facts of one browser (`rootPresent`, `createWritable`, `moveVerified`, `copyVerifyVerified`, `sliceRead`; the middle two are the two replace paths) or no probe at all, *When* `OpfsStorageVerdict.decide(facts)` runs (commonMain, pure), *Then* the rules apply in this order: no probe is `Unavailable(NotProbed)`; no root is `Unavailable(NoOpfs)`; no `createWritable` is `Unavailable(NoCreateWritable)`; neither replace path verified is `Unavailable(NoAtomicReplace)`; no slice read is `Unavailable(NoSliceRead)`; otherwise `Available(Move)` when `moveVerified` and `Available(CopyVerify)` when only `copyVerifyVerified`. Persistence is NOT a fact of the verdict: a denied `navigator.storage.persist()` is a warning (UX-143), never a reason. `OpfsStorageVerdictTest` (commonTest, every target) asserts all 32 combinations of the five booleans against an independently written truth table, which has 16 `NoOpfs`, 8 `NoCreateWritable`, 2 `NoAtomicReplace`, 3 `NoSliceRead`, 2 `Available(Move)` and 1 `Available(CopyVerify)` rows (script-recomputed), plus the `NotProbed` row for the missing probe; a missing fact never yields Available.
- What "durable" means in a browser, and the acknowledgement contract (E1, U1; D-119). *Given* the wasm `PlatformFileSystem`, *Then* a write is `Cached` when it is in the in-memory cache, `Persisted` when the OPFS `createWritable().close()` (or the `move()`) of its final path has resolved, and `Failed` when that promise rejected; a browser gives no `fsync`, so `Persisted` means "the browser committed it to its storage", which survives a reload and a tab crash but not necessarily an operating-system crash or the browser clearing its storage. The synchronous `AtomicFileWriter.write` returns `Right` once the write is `Cached` and queued; the commonMain `FileSystem` gains `suspend fun awaitDurable(path): Either<DomainError, Unit>` (default `Unit.right()`, because the desktop and Android writes are synchronous; declared in Task 1.4.3m so that no non-parked task compiles against this story), the wasm override (Task 1.4.7f) awaits the tail of that path's queue (below) and returns `Left(WriteFailed)` or `Left(StorageQuotaExceeded)` when an operation of the path was rejected (a `QuotaExceededError`, a missing `move()`, a read-back mismatch). `SheetSaver` awaits `SketchDurability.awaitPersisted(path)` after every `Right` and reports `Saved` only then (Task 1.4.7k): between `Cached` and `Persisted` the top-bar status stays "Saving..." (after 3 s it shows the visible `StillSaving` toast "Still saving to this browser." as a polite status message, once, with the timing contract of ux.md 14.8 (at least 1 s visible, never displaces another toast, removed from the list at `Persisted`); the chip's tooltip reads the same sentence for a pointer user but the chip is not a live region, so the toast is the ONE announcement source, UX-140, UX-145, UX-150, D-134, D-144: a tooltip is invisible on touch and a name change is not a status message, WCAG 4.1.3); `Left(StorageQuotaExceeded)` raises the quota variant `SaveFailed:Quota` of the banner (summary "Storage full. Work is kept.", primary [Save copy] (the Export copy action) because Retry cannot succeed on a full store, [Retry] under More, the free-space steps in its Details; the primary stays [Save copy] after an export too; ux.md 14.8, UX-144, D-134) and every other `Left` the existing `SaveFailed` banner ("Couldn't save. Work is kept.", Retry, Export copy); the in-memory scene is never rolled back, and Retry re-enqueues the replace. On `visibilitychange` to hidden the saver skips its debounce and enqueues at once; a `beforeunload` guard prompts while any path is not `Persisted` (a hard tab kill during an in-flight write can still lose that save, never corrupt the previous file: the replace is atomic; the prompt text is the browser's generic one and a mobile browser may not show it at all, so the edits of the last moments, up to one debounce window plus one write, can still be lost there: the `visibilitychange` flush narrows that window and cannot close it), and `pagehide` records the number of unpersisted paths in the log through `Logger`.
- Write ordering (E2; D-120). *Given* the wasm `PlatformFileSystem`, *Then* every OPFS operation (write, delete, move, copy-verify, the mirror writes of `readFileSuspend` and `applyRemoteContent`) goes through ONE `OpfsWriteQueue`: `enqueue(paths, op)` starts `op` only after the queue tails of ALL named paths have settled (a failed predecessor does not block, its failure is kept for `awaitDurable`) and makes the new `Deferred` the tail of each named path, so `replace(tmp, dest)` runs after the temp write and after any earlier write to `dest`, and the temp's own read-back runs after the temp write; `opfsWriteInFlight[path]` becomes that tail (so `opfsWriteDeferredFor` and the host write-through that awaits it wait for every earlier operation too), and an operation removes its entry only while it is still the tail. `OpfsWriteQueueTest` drives three writes to one path and a replace over them with a fake that completes out of order and asserts the settled order, one entry per path and the failure kept.
- Temp, replace and rename side effects (E3; D-121). *Given* `AtomicFileWriter` over the wasm file system, *Then* the temp file is written through `writeTempBytes(path, data)` (a `FileSystem` method defaulting to `writeFileBytes`; the wasm override stores the bytes in `bytesCache` and queues the OPFS write but records NO dirty entry and schedules NO host write-through, so a `.tmp` never reaches git write-back or a linked folder and the "No cached content for dirty path" batch failure cannot arise); `AtomicFileWriter` also removes its temp through `deleteTempBytes(path)` (a `FileSystem` method defaulting to `deleteFile`; the wasm override drops the temp from both caches and queues the OPFS delete but records NO `DirtyOp.DELETE` and no host `Delete`, because `deleteFile` of a never-dirty temp would otherwise delete a file in a linked folder), on every failure path of `write` too (D-137); `replaceFileAtomically(tmp, dest)` moves the temp's bytes to `dest` in the cache (a `.json` sheet as the decoded String in `cache`, every other sidecar file as bytes in `bytesCache`, the stale entry of the other cache removed), removes the temp from both caches and the dirty set, marks `dest` dirty `WRITE` once and schedules ONE host write-through of the final bytes; `renameFile` does the same for byte and text entries (target dirty, source dropped and never recorded as a `DELETE`, one host write-through of the target instead of `renameHostFile`). `PlatformFileSystemDirtyHostTest` (wasmJsBrowserTest, fake host directory) asserts the dirty set, the host payloads and the caches after a temp write, a temp delete, a replace, a rename and an abandoned temp.
- Stale temp files (E4; D-122, D-130). *Given* a `.tmp` older than `StaleTempSweeper.MAX_AGE` left by an earlier page session, *Then* it is removed: `StaleTempSweeper` falls back to the UUIDv7 timestamp in the temp name (`<name>.<uuidv7>.tmp`, the first 48 bits are milliseconds) when `getLastModifiedTime` is null, an unparsable name is skipped, and `sweepTree(root)` recurses over `<slug>/sheets`, `<slug>/conflicts` and `backup/` (`sweepOnce(dir)` is flat per directory, so "sweeps the sidecar root" needs the recursion). The wasm `preload` runs `sweepTree` once per preload under a short-lived lock `stelekit-sketch-sweep:<graphId>` taken with `ifAvailable` (a tab that does not get it skips the sweep); the writer lease below is NOT taken at startup, it is taken only when the first v2 sheet of the graph opens for editing, so the sweep never makes every tab a writer, and another tab's live temp is safe because it is younger than `MAX_AGE`. `StaleTempSweeperTest` (businessTest) covers null time with an old name, null time with a young name, a non-UUID name, a real time and a temp two directories deep. The earlier claim that the sweeper removes a stale temp "on the next write" was false on wasm and is gone.
- Two tabs (E5; D-123). *Given* two tabs on one graph (one OPFS, two preloaded caches, one lock manager), *Then* sheet writes are single-writer per graph: the first tab to open a v2 sheet for editing acquires the lease `stelekit-sketch-writer:<graphId>` through `WebLock.tryAcquireHeld` (a new `ifAvailable` variant of `acquireHeld` that returns a releasable handle, because `tryAcquireLeader` can never be released) and holds it while that graph is the ready graph, releasing it when `GraphManager.readyGraph` moves to another graph or to none (switch, remove, shutdown), so a graph this tab has left can be written from another tab (defence in depth: the driver lock below is held for the life of the tab and a page load opens one graph, so on a persistent web graph no switch happens and the release is exercised only by the unit test with two lease instances over one lock manager); a second tab gets `HeldElsewhere`, opens v2 sheets read-only with the existing `LeaseConflict` banner (its Details name the other tab; the primary action is absent, as for `ReadOnly` in D-99), waits for the lock with `WebLock.acquireHeld` and, when granted, calls `PlatformFileSystem.reloadSidecarRoot()` (the byte preload of the sidecar root, Task 1.4.7b) BEFORE the lease reports `Held` and edits are enabled, so a stale cache can never overwrite a newer file (UX-142). The consumers are Task 1.4.3n (the router: `HeldElsewhere` is the `LeaseConflict` outcome with the reason id `OtherTab`) and Task 1.4.3o (the store refuses the write with `WriterLeaseNotHeld` and the saver goes read-only); the implementation `WasmSketchWriterLease` is Task 1.4.7i, which also constructs it at both wasm `StelekitAppPlatformIntegrations(` sites (D-130, D-146). No whole-file write is last-writer-wins silently. `TwoTabSketchLockTest` (wasmJsBrowserTest; two `PlatformFileSystem` instances in one page share one OPFS and one lock manager, which models two tabs for the lock, the stale cache and the refresh) asserts the second instance is `HeldElsewhere`, its store write is refused with `Failed(WriterLeaseNotHeld)` (the refusal itself is `WriterLeaseRefusalTest`, Task 1.4.3o), after the first releases it reloads the first's bytes before its own write, `readyGraphChange_should_ReleaseTheLeaseAndLetTheOtherInstanceEdit` asserts the release when the ready graph changes, on two lease instances in one page (the state is not reachable from a real second tab); `reacquire_should_ReloadTheSidecarRootAndGrowTheEpoch` asserts the takeover order; verified fact (D-142, D-149): the wasm driver factory already holds `stelekit-sqlite-driver-<graphId>` for the life of the tab (`wasmJsMain/.../db/DriverFactory.js.kt:38`), so a second tab of a persistent OPFS graph stops at boot with `GraphLockedElsewhereException` and shows the boot-refusal screen (UX-152, Task 1.4.6k), never the `OtherTab` banner; this lease is defence in depth for the sidecar files, and Task 1.4.7d records which of the two a real second tab shows; the real two-tab behaviour is an owner step of Task 1.4.7d. `StorageQuotaExceeded` from an OPFS write reaches the saver through `awaitDurable` (above). Eviction cannot be detected after the fact (the registry that would remember a graph is cleared with the same site data), so it is handled by prevention and honesty: `persist()` (UX-143), the first-write sentence (UX-139), and the export copy that the saver offers.
- Preload is a fact of availability (E6; D-124). *Given* `preload` of a graph, *Then* the wasm file system exposes `preloadState` (`NotStarted`, `Loading`, `Ok`, `Failed(cause)`): a missing graph directory (`NotFoundError`) is `Ok` with an empty cache, any other failure while reading the sidecar root (a rejected `getFileHandle`, an unreadable file) is `Failed`, and the text preload keeps today's "start empty" behaviour for the other pages. `V2StorageStatus` (commonMain, pure) combines the verdict and the preload state: `Checking` while the verdict is `NotProbed` or the preload is `NotStarted` or `Loading` (for at most 10 s `[tunable]`), `Ready(sessionOnly)` only for an Available verdict with `preloadState` `Ok` (`sessionOnly` is true for an ephemeral "open temporarily" graph, which never reaches OPFS: its sketches live for the session only), and `Unavailable(reason)` otherwise with `reason` one of the verdict reasons, `PreloadFailed`, `PreloadTooLarge` or `CheckTimedOut`. `supportsTempRenameWrites`, `supportsAtomicReplace` and `ProjectSidecarStore.isAvailable()` are true only for `Ready`, so a failed preload can never let the `NewSketchProject` predicate see "no sheets" and create a v2 sheet over unread data. `OpfsPreloadStateTest` covers the five transitions. **Bound and recovery (E5; D-131).** `SceneLimits.maxPreloadBytes` (128 MB = 134,217,728 bytes, summed over the file sizes of the LIVE files under the sidecar root, `<slug>/project-model.json` and `<slug>/sheets/*.json`, and read from `File.size` BEFORE any byte is read; 500 sheets of `maxSheetBytes` 16 MB could otherwise need about 16 GB as UTF-16 Strings) caps the sidecar-root preload; `backup/`, `conflicts/`, `sources/`, `exports/`, `preview.svg` and temp files are neither summed nor preloaded (D-143): none is read while editing, and `backup/` is never auto-pruned (D-6), so counting it would let a healthy project reach `PreloadTooLarge` and make the new editor permanently `Unavailable` on web with the "Delete backups" remedy unreachable; they are listed by name and read on demand through `readFileBytesSuspend`: over the cap the state is `Failed(PreloadTooLarge)`, the status `Unavailable(PreloadTooLarge)`, nothing is read, and the reason text names the size and the cap. `Loading` covers ONLY the sidecar-root byte preload (the text preload of the other pages goes on in the background and never holds the status, because the `NewSketchProject` predicate reads only sidecar files). A preload that finishes `Ok` within 60 s `[tunable]` after the 10 s `CheckTimedOut` recovers once: the status flips to `Ready` (the mode control and an open `Pending` or no-editor screen re-evaluate, no reload is needed) and a later `Failed` is ignored; a `Failed` finish, or an `Ok` later than 60 s, leaves it `Unavailable` and the screen offers [Reload]. `OpfsPreloadStateTest` adds `overTheCap_should_BePreloadTooLarge_AndReadNothing` (live files over the cap), `projectWithHugeBackupDir_should_StayAvailable`, `backupConflictsAndSources_should_NotBeReadByThePreload`, `lateOkWithinSixtySeconds_should_RecoverOnce`, `lateOkAfterSixtySeconds_should_StayUnavailable` and `textPreload_should_NotHoldTheStatus`.
- Persistence request and warnings (E7, U1; D-125, D-128). *Given* a first v2 write in a web graph, *Then* `navigator.storage.persist()` is requested lazily inside the click handler of the first-write dialog ([Create and open] or [Convert and open], a user gesture) and never at startup, because a browser may show a prompt for it (INFERRED for Firefox; Spike 0.1.8 records whether each browser prompts), through the `BrowserPersistence` port of Task 1.4.3k so that the commonMain dialog of Task 1.4.6a never compiles against `opfsRequestPersist` (the adapter `WasmBrowserPersistence` over it is Task 1.4.7e, which also constructs it at both wasm sites, D-146); the answer is `PersistenceState` (`Granted`, `Denied`, `NotRequested`, `Unsupported`), shown in Settings > Sketches as "Browser storage" with [Ask the browser to keep it] while it is not `Granted`; `Denied` shows the one non-modal notice of UX-143 and changes no availability; a click on [Ask the browser to keep it] shows an inline status line on its row while it waits ("Asking the browser...") and, when the answer is `Denied`, the refusal line ("The browser said no. ...", ux.md 14.8, UX-147), so a refused click is never silent.
- Bytes, prefixes, listing (E9; D-126). *Given* the wasm `PlatformFileSystem`, *Then* `readFileBytes` returns the bytes of `bytesCache` or the UTF-8 bytes of `cache` (null when absent, never throws); `readFilePrefix(path, maxBytes)` (the interface method of Task 1.4.3g) takes `min(maxBytes, length)` characters of a cached String (each character is at least one byte, so that is always enough), encodes only that slice and truncates it to `maxBytes`, never encoding the whole multi-megabyte String (the prefix may end inside a multi-byte character and callers treat it as bytes); `renameFile` moves byte and text entries (today text only, `wasmJsMain/.../PlatformFileSystem.kt:610-618`), and `fileExists`, `listFiles`, `listDirectories` and `directoryExists` also see byte entries (today `cache` only, `wasmJsMain/.../PlatformFileSystem.kt:491-501`); `preload` reads the LIVE files of the sidecar root (`assets/stelekit-sketch`; the live set of D-143: each project's model and sheets) into the caches (the existing text preload is lossless for the UTF-8 JSON files and lossy for anything else) and lists every other file by name only. After `preload` every read of a live file is a cache read, and a read of any other sidecar file goes through `readFileBytesSuspend`.
- Durable in a real browser, and the budget (E8). *Given* `FileProjectSidecarStore` over the real wasm `PlatformFileSystem` in `:kmp:wasmJsBrowserTest` (Chrome), *When* a 1 MB sheet is written, `awaitDurable` resolves, the in-memory caches are dropped and `preload` runs again, *Then* `read` returns the same bytes; *When* a write is interrupted between the temp write and the replace, *Then* `read` returns the previous bytes; no write returns `AtomicWriteUnsupported`; and 50 `readFilePrefix(path, 256)` calls over 50 cached 2 MB sheets take under 10 ms in total (the time of the prefix reads themselves, measured here; the `NewSketchProject` predicate of Task 1.4.3i adds no more than the model read, and its own timing lives in `NewSketchProjectPredicateTest`).
- A pending verdict is a state, not a failure (U2; D-128, UX-141). *Given* the mode control, a v2 embed or a v2 sheet is shown while `V2StorageStatus` is `Checking`, *Then* the mode control shows its two On options disabled with "Checking whether this browser can save new-editor sketches..." (a polite live region; Off stays enabled), an embed shows its labelled loading placeholder, opening a v2 sheet shows the router's `Pending` state ("Checking this browser's storage...", [Close]) instead of the no-editor screen, a create action waits for the status (cancellable) and then follows the resolved row (it never creates a v2 sheet from `Checking`), and after 10 s the status becomes `Unavailable(CheckTimedOut)` with its own reason text and [Reload]. The `Pending` state has the focus and announcement contract of the table `pending-contract` in ux.md 14.8 (UX-146; WCAG 2.4.3, 4.1.3): focus on its heading on arrival, a polite message at 5 s, focus on the editor canvas or the no-editor heading when it resolves, and the no-editor screen of `CheckTimedOut` at 10 s; `NoEditorAccessibilityTest` (Task 1.4.6j) and `EditorRouterTest` (Task 1.4.3d) assert its rows. `WebStorageUxTest` covers the status-to-notice mapping, the tailored reason of each `Unavailable` reason and of an iOS WebKit browser, and the session-only wording.
- Web browser pass (Task 1.4.7d). *Given* Chrome, Firefox and Safari (owner-run; the browsers whose Spike 0.1.8 verdict is Available), *When* the owner creates, edits, saves, reloads, imports a `.excalidraw` by file picker and by drop, embeds, copies a v2 sketch, opens a desktop-synced v2 sheet (with the setting `Off`, found by the discovery scan), opens the same graph in two tabs and times the preload of a 500-sheet project, and then repeats the classic steps once with the classic file side turned on in the console (`localStorage.setItem('sketch.web.classicFileSide', 'On')`, then a reload), *Then* each step passes or is recorded in `owner-verification.md` with the browser and its verdict; a browser whose verdict is Unavailable is checked for the classic-only state instead, and the owner records whether such a browser could still READ a sheet (the claim of section 14.7 that a sheet it cannot save also cannot be exported is a limit of this release, not a measured fact, until that row exists).
- Whole-option abandonment (product gap; D-127). *Given* Spike 0.1.8 records neither Chrome nor Firefox as Available, *Then* Story 1.4.7 and every task of it is recorded `Skipped(opfs-spike)` in `implementation/parked.md`, D-104 is re-recorded by the owner, the web build stays classic-only in every browser, and the parked set `OPFSSPIKE` of the plan-parked block applies (the web browser pass, Task 1.4.7d, is skipped with the rest, and the reduced form of Task 6.2.5b lists the skipped nodes and omits that pass; metric 6 loses its wasmJs part and metric 8 loses its web part); the shared storage ports of Story 1.4.3 (set `OPFSSHARED`, Tasks 1.4.3k to 1.4.3r) stay built because no non-parked task depends on a parked one, and the spike's own cost is spent. **Demand check (D-139).** *Given* Spike 0.1.8 records Chrome or Firefox as Available but no browser family recorded by the pre-check items or the web intent is Available (for example only a Firefox item was counted and Firefox failed while Chrome passed), *Then* the same recorded outcome applies (`Skipped(opfs-spike)`, the owner re-records D-104, the spike's cost only) unless the owner records that web is wanted without a counted item; a Safari verdict alone never abandons Option W and a Safari-only item never keeps it. The rule is the `abandon-cases` block of requirements.md, recomputed by `check-plan.py` (it is not code).
- Option W keep or drop rule (product; D-133, D-138, D-139). *Given* Task 0.1.7c has recorded the list of the next 5 diagrams with each item's platform (the SteleKit platform it WOULD be authored on, recorded for an "elsewhere" item too) and, for `web`, its browser families (in the OWNER0 session, before W1), *When* `webItems` is the number of items whose platform is `web` and whose families include `chromium` or `firefox` (the mark does not matter; a `safari`-only item is not counted; the count is provisional, before any OPFS verdict exists), *Then* Option W is KEPT when `webItems >= 1`, or when `webItems = 0` and the owner records `web-intent: yes -- <named use>, <family>` in `baselines.md` (a use is required and `safari` alone does not count), and DROPPED otherwise; the parameters and the eleven worked cases are the `keep-drop` and `keep-drop-cases` blocks of requirements.md, which `check-plan.py` recomputes. Dropping records Tasks 0.1.8a and 0.1.8b and every task of this story `Skipped(opfs-spike)` before W1 (the sets `OPFSDROP` and `OPFSSPIKE` of the plan-parked block, the same recorded outcome as a failed spike), keeps the web build classic-only (the new editor off; classic sketches keep persisting in the database, D-140), has the owner re-record D-104 and saves the computed Option W cost (requirements.md "Web first release: what works" quotes it with its share of the total and the total web footprint). The count is provisional until Task 0.1.8c re-reads it at the end of W1 (D-148): a web item counted provisionally that the spike voids can turn a PASS into a recount FAIL, which the owner then answers with `continue` or `stop` before any W2 node starts; after that re-read the recount is information only and never reopens the decision; the label that names the record when several stages apply is the `label-precedence` block.
- Private windows where OPFS works (U5; D-136, UX-148; INFERRED). *Given* a browser whose verdict is Available while its window is private (Chromium incognito and Safari private browsing keep the OPFS API and clear it when the window closes), *Then* the probe records `navigator.storage.estimate().quota` (Task 1.4.7e) and `PrivateWindowHint.from(quota)` (Task 1.4.3k, pure) is `privateSuspected` when the quota is below `EPHEMERAL_QUOTA_BYTES` = 268,435,456 (256 MiB) and `persist()` was not granted. The heuristic is INFERRED: Spike 0.1.8 records the estimate in a normal and in a private window of each browser, and where the two do not differ the hint is dropped and only the conservative wording stays. When `privateSuspected` and the graph is not opened temporarily, `Ready` carries it, the first-write dialog shows the private-window line of ux.md 14.2 instead of the durability line, the status chip's tooltip and accessible name for Saved read "Saved in this window. A private window may clear it when it closes.", and the hint row shows a persistent cue, or the chip row does in a window without `HintStatus` (ux.md 14.8, D-155; a touch user never sees a tooltip and sees the dialog once); when the graph IS opened temporarily the session-only wording wins in all those places, the cue included (a temporary graph has its own persistent cue), because that graph never reaches OPFS and the private-window question does not arise (the `durability-wording` block of ux.md 14.8 is the one table, `WebStorageUx.durabilityWording(sessionOnly, privateSuspected)` is pure, UX-151); where `persist()` is `Unsupported` the durability line is the conservative one. False positives are possible (a normal window on a device with little free disk can report a low quota): the wording says "looks like", and the hint is dropped when the spike shows no separation between a normal and a private window (the per-browser decision goes into `research/opfs-spike.md`). `V2StorageStatusTest` asserts the threshold boundary (one byte below, at the threshold) and `WebStorageUxTest` the three wordings.
- The startup probe has a place and cleans up (D-137). *Given* `OpfsCapability` runs on every page load, *Then* it works only inside `stelekit-probe-<uuidv7>` directly under the OPFS root (never under a graph or the sidecar root, so no listing, sweep or preload sees it), writes at most 3 files of at most 64 bytes each (create, move, copy-verify; the slice read reuses one of them), deletes the whole directory in a `finally`, and removes any other `stelekit-probe-*` directory whose UUIDv7 is more than one day old (a crashed probe); two tabs probing at once use two directories and never share a path. `OpfsAtomicReplaceTest` asserts the directory name, the 3 x 64 byte bound, the cleanup after a failure and two concurrent probes.
- Composition roots and the `plan-wiring` table (E1, E2; D-132, D-141, D-146). *Given* the seams of Stories 1.4.3, 1.4.7 and 2.1.2, *Then* each implementation is constructed in a composition-root file by a task that lists that file, and the table `plan-wiring` (Dependency Visualization) has one row per seam implementation: `SketchWriterLease` (`AlwaysHeldSketchWriterLease`, Task 1.4.3p, catalog lines in Task 1.4.3q; `WasmSketchWriterLease`, Task 1.4.7i), `BrowserPersistence` (`UnsupportedBrowserPersistence`, Tasks 1.4.3p and 1.4.3q; `WasmBrowserPersistence`, Task 1.4.7e), `SketchDurability` (`FileSystemSketchDurability`, Task 1.4.7m), `PageLifecycle` (`WasmPageLifecycle`, Task 1.4.7m: the `visibilitychange` to hidden event becomes the flush request that `SheetSaver` collects) and `TextMetricsProvider` (`ComposeTextMetricsProvider`, Task 2.1.2b, catalog lines in Task 2.1.2c). `OpfsCapability` is a class, not an interface: `WiringCatalog` rejects a seam that is not on `WiringAudit.SEAMS` and `findImplementations` skips a class whose own name is on it, so it has no row and no catalog line; the wasm `Main.kt` starts the probe once per page load and `OpfsAtomicReplaceTest` covers it through its consumer, the verdict. **The order that keeps the audit green in every wave (D-146):** each interface is added to `WiringAudit.SEAMS` by the task that constructs its first implementation (Task 1.4.3p, 2.1.2b or 1.4.7m, together with the SEAMS list assertion of `WiringCompletenessTest`, which the audit does not update by itself); an implementation declared later is constructed by the task that declares it (`WasmBrowserPersistence` in Task 1.4.7e and `WasmSketchWriterLease` in Task 1.4.7i, both at the two wasm `StelekitAppPlatformIntegrations(` sites, after Task 1.4.3t made the fields required); a catalog line is added only by a task after the list task. A `wiring-catalog.txt` line needs an entry-point test in `androidUnitTest` or `jvmTest` that calls `WiredAppFixture.` or `DesktopWiredAppFixture.` and `ObservableEffect.assert(label)`; `wasmJsTest` is not bundled and has no fixture, so the four wasm implementations have no catalog line, are audited for construction (the wasm `browser/Main.kt` root is in the bundle) and are behave-tested by `WebStorageWiringTest` (D-141; the priced alternative is a wasm fixture plus the bundle). The `plan-seam-classes` block lists the nine names that are not seams.
- Classic sketches on the web build (P1, P2; D-140, D-147, D-153; Tasks 1.4.7o and 1.4.7n). *Given* a browser whose verdict is Available and the setting `sketch.web.classicFileSide` is `Off` (the default), *Then* every classic sheet behaves exactly as before Option W, including the classic sheets of a project that also holds a v2 sheet: `SidecarReconciler` reports `DeviceLocal`, nothing of a classic sheet is written under the sidecar root, `SketchExporter` returns `AtomicWriteUnsupported`, no `preview.svg` is written and the database stays authoritative (`WasmClassicReconcileTest.settingOff_should_LeaveEveryClassicFileSideUntouched`, `ClassicWebFileSidePolicyTest.classicSheetOfAMixedProject_should_StayDatabaseOnlyUnderOff`); *Given* the setting `Off`, the verdict Available and a v2 project created on desktop and synced by git that the web database does not know, *Then* the discovery scan finds it and its v2 sheets open editable, a classic-only synced project is left untouched, and a mixed project shows its v2 sheets only (the rows of the `classic-web-scenarios` block; `SyncedV2ProjectDiscoveryTest`, `WasmClassicReconcileTest.settingOffAndSyncedV2Project_should_OpenEditable`); *Given* the setting is `On` and the verdict Available, *Then* the classic file side runs (write-through of the bundle, pull, `importNew`, `replaceProjectContents`, exports, `preview.svg`), and the three cases that exist only because wasm gains a file side are tested on the wasm file system: an existing web database plus a desktop-synced bundle of the same slug (`existingDatabaseAndSyncedBundle_should_ReconcileLikeDesktop`), divergent edits (`divergentEdits_should_FollowTheDesktopOutcomeRows`) and the database overwrite of a pull (`pullOverwrite_should_ReplaceTheDatabaseContentsOnlyThroughReplaceProjectContents`); the policy is the pure `ClassicWebFileSidePolicy` (`ClassicWebFileSidePolicyTest`: `verdictUnavailable_should_BeOffWhateverTheSetting`, `defaultSetting_should_BeOff`, `v2Sheet_should_BeReconciledRegardlessOfTheSetting`); the setting has no UI in this release, its owner switch is the console line of Task 1.4.7d, and its default is the owner's release decision (D-147).
- The gate precedes the availability switch (E1; D-154). *Given* the edge block, *Then* Task 1.4.7o is an ancestor of every task of the `plan-availability-switch` block in an earlier wave, and `ClassicWebFileSidePolicyTest.defaultSetting_should_BeOff` is green before Task 1.4.7j starts, so no wave runs the classic file side ungated on wasm.
**Files**: `CM/platform/OpfsStorageVerdict.kt` (new, pure; the types it returns are in Task 1.4.3k), `CM/platform/AtomicFileWriter.kt`, `CM/platform/StaleTempSweeper.kt`, `CM/projectbridge/SketchDurability.kt` (new), `CM/ui/sketch/WebStorageUx.kt` (new), `kmp/src/wasmJsMain/kotlin/dev/stapler/stelekit/platform/OpfsCapability.kt` (new, the probe), `kmp/src/wasmJsMain/kotlin/dev/stapler/stelekit/platform/WebLock.kt` (`tryAcquireHeld`), `kmp/src/wasmJsMain/kotlin/dev/stapler/stelekit/platform/OpfsInterop.kt`, `kmp/src/wasmJsMain/kotlin/dev/stapler/stelekit/platform/WasmBrowserPersistence.kt` (new), `kmp/src/wasmJsMain/kotlin/dev/stapler/stelekit/platform/OpfsWriteQueue.kt` (new), `kmp/src/wasmJsMain/kotlin/dev/stapler/stelekit/platform/WasmSketchWriterLease.kt` (new), `kmp/src/wasmJsMain/kotlin/dev/stapler/stelekit/platform/WasmPageLifecycle.kt` (new), `kmp/src/wasmJsMain/kotlin/dev/stapler/stelekit/platform/PlatformFileSystem.kt`, the wasm `browser/Main.kt`, the tests of the tasks below, and `owner-verification.md`; the `FileSystem` defaults, `SketchWriterLease` and `V2StorageStatus` are NOT here (Stories 1.4.3, Tasks 1.4.3k to 1.4.3m)
##### Task 1.4.7a: `OpfsStorageVerdict` (pure, commonMain; returns `StorageReason` of Task 1.4.3k) and the status reducer from verdict, `preloadState` and elapsed time to `V2StorageStatus` with `OpfsStorageVerdictTest` (the 32-row truth table and the status transitions), registered in the commonTest `BUILD.bazel` srcs and `AllBusinessTests` (S); after Task 1.4.3k
##### Task 1.4.7e: The OPFS primitives (`opfsMoveFile`, `opfsCopyVerify`, `opfsReadSlice`, `opfsRequestPersist`, the typed mapping of `QuotaExceededError` to `StorageQuotaExceeded`), `WasmBrowserPersistence` over `opfsRequestPersist`, constructed at both wasm `StelekitAppPlatformIntegrations(` sites in the same task so the audit never sees an unconstructed implementation (D-146), and the startup probe `OpfsCapability` (a class, not a `WiringAudit` seam, D-141) that fills the five facts and the quota estimate, in its own directory, with `OpfsAtomicReplaceTest` (M); after Tasks 1.4.7a, 1.4.3l and 1.4.3t

##### Task 1.4.7b: wasm `PlatformFileSystem` byte API (byte reads, the character-bounded prefix read, byte-aware rename, listing and existence, the byte preload of the LIVE files of the sidecar root (D-143), the on-demand `readFileBytesSuspend` override and `reloadSidecarRoot()`) with `PlatformFileSystemBytesTest` (M); after Task 1.4.7f
##### Task 1.4.7g: `AtomicFileWriter` writes and deletes its temp through `writeTempBytes` and `deleteTempBytes`, with the dirty-set and host write-through semantics on wasm and `PlatformFileSystemDirtyHostTest` (S); after Task 1.4.7b
##### Task 1.4.7h: `StaleTempSweeper` UUIDv7 fallback for a null modification time and the recursive `sweepTree(root)`, with `StaleTempSweeperTest`, registered in `AllBusinessTests` (S); after Task 1.4.3g
##### Task 1.4.7i: `WasmSketchWriterLease` over a new releasable `WebLock.tryAcquireHeld` implementing the port of Task 1.4.3m (acquire on the first v2 sheet opened for editing, release when the graph stops being the ready graph, the takeover, `reloadSidecarRoot()` and a new `epoch` before `Held`; D-142, D-150), constructed at both wasm `StelekitAppPlatformIntegrations(` sites in the same task (D-146), with `TwoTabSketchLockTest` (M); after Tasks 1.4.7e, 1.4.7b and 1.4.3m
##### Task 1.4.7j: `preloadState`, the `V2StorageStatus` wiring that replaces the interim override of Task 1.4.3m (`supportsTempRenameWrites`, `supportsAtomicReplace`, `storageStatus`), the live-set over-the-cap and late-recovery rules (`SceneLimits.maxPreloadBytes` is already in the 32-field list of Task 1.2.4a and is only read here, D-145), the once-per-preload `sweepTree` under its short-lived sweep lock and `OpfsPreloadStateTest`(this task is the one that can make wasm storage available, so the classic file side gate of Task 1.4.7o is already installed, D-154) (M); after Tasks 1.4.7a, 1.4.7g, 1.4.7h, 1.4.7i and 1.4.7o

##### Task 1.4.7l: `WebStorageUx` (the pure status-to-notice and reason-to-text mapping, the quota variant, the `StillSaving` toast rule, the pure `durabilityWording(sessionOnly, privateSuspected)` over the four-row table of ux.md 14.8 (UX-151), the two durability cues with their four forms (block `cue-forms`), the line-sharing rule and the chip form chosen per window without `HintStatus` (UX-153, D-151, D-155), the temporary-graph wordings and cue, the `save-status-names` block, the `StillSaving` timing contract (UX-150) and the refusal line) and `WebStorageUxTest`, registered in the commonTest `BUILD.bazel` srcs and `AllBusinessTests` (S); after Task 1.4.7j and Story 1.4.6

# Phase 2: Rendering

### Epic 2.1: Draw list and Clean renderer
**Goal**: One deterministic draw list drives screen, SVG and PNG.

#### Story 2.1.1: DrawOp builders for shapes, lines, arrows, frames, images
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Golden draw lists.
  - *Given* the fixture "labelled box + bound arrow + frame", *When* `DrawListBuilder.build(scene, Clean)` runs with the fixed-advance `TextMetricsProvider`, *Then* the output equals `golden/architecture-15.drawlist.json` and is identical on JVM and wasmJs.
- Frame clipping and layer opacity.
  - *Given* a child extending past its `FrameElement`, *When* built, *Then* the child ops are wrapped in a clip op; and a layer opacity 0.5 with element opacity 0.5 yields effective 0.25.
**Files**: `V2/render/DrawOp.kt`, `V2/render/DrawListBuilder.kt`, `V2/render/ArrowheadGeometry.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/DrawListGoldenTest.kt`
##### Task 2.1.1a: DrawOp + builder for rect/diamond/ellipse/line/polyline incl. roundness and dash, plus `PointElement` (small cross/dot marker) and `ArcElement` (arc path, Clean and Sketchy) when OD-8 is accepted (M)
##### Task 2.1.1b: Arrow curves + the 9 v1 arrowheads (M)
##### Task 2.1.1c: Frame clip, image op, layer opacity, per-element cache key `(id, version)` (S)

#### Story 2.1.2: Text metrics, bundled fonts, wrapping
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Persisted size and wrap points are authoritative; remeasure only on edit (pre-mortem P2 #7).
  - *Given* a `TextElement` with stored width 96, stored `lines` and the font unloaded, *When* rendered, *Then* no remeasure happens, layout uses the stored width/height and the stored line breaks, and no measured size is persisted while font status is not loaded.
  - *Given* the same sheet bytes opened on JVM and on wasmJs with different measured advances, *When* the draw list is built, *Then* the text ops are equal (a cross-target golden over one fixed sheet; wrap points are a WRITE-TIME decision stored in the element, so another device never re-wraps); only an edit re-measures and re-wraps.
- Wrapping deterministic.
  - *Given* text "Authentication Service" at width 120 with the fixed-advance provider (8 units/char, so 15 chars per line), *When* wrapped, *Then* the lines are `Authentication` and `Service` and identical across targets.
- CJK and missing glyphs follow the Spike 0.1.3b policy.
  - *Given* "データ" in a `TextElement`, *When* measured, *Then* the persisted per-element width/height are used for layout, wrap points for CJK are not asserted across targets (unless the spike chose a bundled CJK subset), and rendering never shows tofu boxes without a fallback (a test with a fake provider lacking the glyph asserts the fallback path is taken).
**Files**: `V2/text/TextMetricsProvider.kt`, `UI2/ComposeTextMetricsProvider.kt`, `CM/composeResources/font/*` (fonts per OD-4), `CT/sketch/v2/TextWrapTest.kt` (includes the persisted-size/wrap cross-target golden)
##### Task 2.1.2a: Seam, fake, wrap algorithm (S)
##### Task 2.1.2b: Compose `TextMeasurer` adapter + font bundle + wasm font-load invalidation + construction of `ComposeTextMetricsProvider` in `StelekitAppDependencies.kt`, which adds `TextMetricsProvider` to `WiringAudit.SEAMS` and to the SEAMS list assertion of `WiringCompletenessTest` (8 names after this task; D-146) (M); after Task 2.1.2a
##### Task 2.1.2c: The entry-point tests of `ComposeTextMetricsProvider`, `TextMetricsAndroidEntryPointTest` (`WiredAppFixture`) and `TextMetricsDesktopEntryPointTest` (`DesktopWiredAppFixture`), their two `wiring-catalog.txt` lines and their registration in `AllAndroidUnitTests` and `AllJvmTests` (S); its own node, after Task 2.1.2b

#### Story 2.1.3: Compose painter with static and dynamic layers
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Dragging one element does not recompose others.
  - *Given* a 500-element scene, *When* one element is dragged for 60 pointer moves, *Then* the recomposition counter for the static layer stays at 0 and the dynamic layer recomposes.
- Frame time budget.
  - *Given* 500 elements, *When* the JVM CI benchmark pans for 120 frames, *Then* p95 frame time is at most 1.5x the empty-canvas p95 measured in the same run (one relative rule; CI-enforced via `sketch-engine-budgets.json`). The Pixel_Fold AVD p95 (same 1.5x rule, 24 ms absolute ceiling) is an OWNER-RUN advisory gate recorded in `owner-verification.md` (Story 6.2.4c), never a CI condition.
- Per-target performance pivot (requirements, Abort and pivot criteria; Task 6.2.9c). *Given* the owner-run Pixel_Fold or the wasmJs p95 for 500 elements exceeds both 1.5x the Task 0.1.7b empty-canvas reference and the 24 ms ceiling, *Then* the static layer cache and culling are confirmed on, `SceneLimits.softElementThreshold` is applied lower for that target only, and the supported element count for that target is recorded in the release notes; the JVM/desktop release is never blocked by it.
**Files**: `UI2/SketchCanvasV2.kt`, `UI2/ScenePainter.kt`, `UI2/PathCache.kt`, `JT/sketch/v2/ScenePainterTest.kt` (`// jvmTest-reason: pixels`)
##### Task 2.1.3a: Painter consuming DrawOps with viewport culling via index (M)
##### Task 2.1.3b: Static cache layer + dynamic overlay; recomposition-count test (M)
##### Task 2.1.3c: Pixel tolerance fixtures JVM/wasm, plus Robolectric only if Spike 0.1.3e found it reliable (else Android parity = draw-list equality + owner on-device check) (S)

#### Story 2.1.4: Freedraw smoothing (perfect-freehand clean-room port)
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Matches the JS golden.
  - *Given* the 30-point fixture with seed-independent pressures from Spike 0.1.5, *When* `FreehandOutline.build` runs, *Then* every outline point is within 1e-6 of `freehand-golden.json`.
- Point cap.
  - *Given* a 20,000-point stroke, *When* committed, *Then* Ramer-Douglas-Peucker leaves at most 2,000 points and the stored element is under 64 KB.
**Files**: `V2/render/FreehandOutline.kt`, `V2/render/PointSimplifier.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/FreehandGoldenTest.kt`

##### Task 2.1.4b: RDP simplification + pressure optional (`simulatePressure`) (S)

### Epic 2.2: Sketchy style
**Goal**: Hand-drawn rendering, deterministic across targets (owner decision).

#### Story 2.2.1: Seeded PRNG and rough subset (line, rect, ellipse, polygon, curve)
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- PRNG and ops match JS goldens.
  - *Given* seed 1337 and a 100x60 `RectElement` with roughness 1, *When* `SketchyStrokeStyle` generates ops, *Then* they equal `rough-golden.json` within 1e-6 on JVM and wasmJs.
- Roughness 0 renders Clean.
  - *Given* roughness 0, *Then* the draw list equals the Clean list byte for byte. Style seam default per new sheet follows OD-5.
**Files**: `V2/render/SketchyStrokeStyle.kt`, `V2/render/CleanStrokeStyle.kt`, `V2/render/SeededRandom.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/SketchyGoldenTest.kt`
##### Task 2.2.1a: `StrokeStyleProvider` seam + Clean (XS)
##### Task 2.2.1b: PRNG + double-stroke/bow subset port with MIT notice (M)
##### Task 2.2.1c: Seed persistence and per-sheet default setting (S)

#### Story 2.2.2: Fills (hachure, cross-hatch, zigzag, solid) and import fidelity
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Hachure fill clipped to shape.
  - *Given* a diamond with `fillStyle=hachure`, gap 8, *When* built, *Then* all hatch segments lie inside the diamond polygon (property test) and the count equals the golden.
- Unknown fill falls back to solid on import and is retained in `extras`.
**Files**: `V2/render/HachureFill.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/HachureFillTest.kt`
##### Task 2.2.2a: Hachure/cross-hatch/zigzag generators + clipping (M)
##### Task 2.2.2b: Fill style plumbing to style panel model (XS)

#### Story 2.2.3: wasmJs/iOS rendering checks and degradation
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Sketchy renders on wasmJs.
  - *Given* the "architecture-15" scene in Sketchy on `:kmp:wasmJsBrowserTest`, *When* rendered offscreen, *Then* pixel diff against the JVM render is within the tolerance set in Task 2.1.3c; iOS compiles (`compileKotlinIosArm64`).
- Cap degradation, ONE threshold (UX-73; resolves the 4,900 vs 4,000 mismatch).
  - *Given* a scene with 4,000 elements (`SceneLimits.softElementThreshold`, 80% of the 5,000 cap) or 160 constraints (`softConstraintThreshold`), *When* opened or when the count crosses it, *Then* the `LargeDrawing` candidate (ux.md banner table; this story emits it) "Large drawing: effects off." with [Learn more] and Dismiss under More shows (Details: "Sketchy style is off, shadows are skipped, live constraint glyphs are hidden. Your drawing is unchanged." [Re-enable effects]) and degradation follows the UX order (Sketchy drawn as Clean with the stored `roughness` untouched, then shadows, culling, low-res thumbnails, constraint glyphs); at 3,999 elements no banner shows; "Re-enable effects" turns them back on for the session. The hard cap (5,000 / 200) actions are Story 2.2.4.
- Sketchy availability per target (requirements, Abort and pivot criteria; Task 6.2.9a). *Given* a target whose Sketchy render of architecture-15 misses the metric-4 tolerance, or whose Sketchy draw-list build for 500 elements takes more than 2x the Clean build on the same host, *Then* a per-target `SketchyAvailability` entry makes `StrokeStyleProvider` resolve to Clean there (stored `roughness` and `seed` untouched) with the notice "Hand-drawn style is not available on this device yet", and ADR-004 records it.
**Files**: `UI2/LargeSceneDegradation.kt`, wasm test counterpart, `JT/sketch/v2/LargeSceneDegradationTest.kt` (new, `// jvmTest-reason: pixels`)
##### Task 2.2.3a: wasm pixel check + iOS compile (S)
##### Task 2.2.3b: Degradation policy + banner at the soft thresholds (S)

#### Story 2.2.4: Capacity UX at the hard cap (UX-73, consistency N5; needs 2.2.3)
**As a** user at the scene cap, **I want** a way forward when drawing is refused, **so that** the limit is never a dead end.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Hard cap actions.
  - *Given* a scene at `SceneLimits.maxElements` (5,000), *When* the user draws or pastes a new element, *Then* nothing is added and the message "This sketch has 5,000 elements, the maximum." shows with actions Delete some, Split into a new sketch and OK; *Given* a selection of 40 elements, *When* "Split into a new sketch" is chosen, *Then* a new v2 sheet is created in the same project containing exactly those elements (ids kept, bindings to unselected elements cleared and reported, constraints among the moved elements carried) as one operation, the originals are deleted from the source sheet as ONE undoable step in the SOURCE sheet's own `HistoryV2`, and the source and new sheet both read `Present` afterwards (new-sheet creation obeys the `ConversionPolicy` rule of Story 1.1.4).
- Atomicity across two sheet files (engineering gap 7). There is no cross-sheet undo, because history is per sheet and the saver is single-writer. Mechanism: under ONE `SidecarSlugLock` hold, `SheetSplitter` (1) writes the new sheet file as a staged unreferenced `sheets/<new>.v2.json`, (2) writes the source sheet's new bytes, (3) performs ONE model write (atomic rename) that adds the new `sheetRef` and updates the source `fileSha256` together, and (4) updates the DB rows through `DatabaseWriteActor`. The model write is the commit point: a crash before it leaves the project reading `Present` with the source unchanged and a staged unreferenced file that the converter-style cleanup removes; a crash after it leaves both sheets present. *When* the user undoes the split in the source sheet, *Then* the 40 elements return to the source, and a notice offers "Delete the new sketch '<name>'" (the new sheet is never removed silently, and undo of the source does not delete user data). `SheetSplitterTest` injects a crash after each of steps (1)-(4). Mode intent: the new sheet is v2 in every mode in which the source sheet is editable (it is only reachable from an editable v2 sheet, and that project already needs a v2-capable reader), so `NewProjectsOnly` allows a v2 sheet from a v2 sheet and creates no new unreadable project; `SheetSplitterTest` asserts it under `NewProjectsOnly`, and `FlagModeMatrixTest` asserts the router's new-sheet rule is not consulted for it.
  - *Given* 200 constraints, *When* a 201st is added, *Then* the `ConstraintLimit` toast "Constraint limit reached." (Details: "200 constraints is the limit for one sketch.") offers Review and Details (Story 4.2.1 carries the command-level cap).
- Low memory (Android).
  - *Given* an `OutOfMemoryError` caught by the editor guard, *Then* the `LowMemory` toast "Low memory." shows with Save now and, under More, Dismiss (Details: "Save and reopen the sketch.") and the scene stays in memory (`SketchErrorState.LowMemory`).
**Files**: `UI2/CapacityMessages.kt`, `RT/split/SheetSplitter.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/SheetSplitterTest.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/CapacityUxTest.kt`
##### Task 2.2.4a: Hard-cap message and actions plus `SheetSplitter` with the two-sheet one-model-write mechanism, binding clearing, constraint carry and `ConversionPolicy` (M); reclassified from S
##### Task 2.2.4b: Constraint-cap message and Android low-memory message (XS)
