# Phase 3: Tools and UX

### Epic 3.1: Tool framework and input
**Goal**: Pure tool machines, normalised input, adaptive tool strip.

#### Story 3.1.1: `Tool`, `InputEvent`, scripted-scenario harness
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Cancel and second-pointer rules.
  - *Given* the Rectangle tool mid-drag, *When* a second pointer goes down, *Then* the preview is cancelled with no command and the tool returns to Idle; *When* Esc arrives mid-drag, *Then* the same.
- Tool parity is guarded (success metric 1; the inventory is frozen at the 14 tools listed in requirements.md metric 1, so adding or removing a tool is a requirements change, not an implementation choice).
  - *Given* the `Tool` registry (select, rectangle, diamond, ellipse, arrow, line, freedraw, text, image, eraser, frame, hand, and point and arc when OD-8 is accepted), *When* `ToolInventoryCompletenessTest` enumerates it, *Then* every tool id appears in at least one scenario test.
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-08 a second pointer or Esc cancels the preview with no element and no undo entry.
**Files**: `V2/tools/Tool.kt`, `V2/tools/InputEvent.kt`, `V2/tools/ToolContext.kt`, `V2/tools/ToolRegistry.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/tools/ToolScenarioHarness.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/tools/ToolCancelTest.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/tools/ToolInventoryCompletenessTest.kt` (new)
##### Task 3.1.1a: Interfaces + `ToolResult(command?, preview?, next?)` (S)
##### Task 3.1.1b: Scenario DSL (events in, commands out) used by every tool test (S)

#### Story 3.1.2: Pointer adapter and navigation
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Touch navigates, stylus/mouse acts.
  - *Given* a `PointerKind.Touch` one-finger drag in Rectangle mode, *When* released, *Then* the view panned and no element was created; *Given* `Stylus` the same drag creates one `RectElement`; the override toggle "Finger drawing" ON makes touch draw (UX-07).
- Finger drawing is ONE named setting with a defined default (U1, UX-80; owner-confirmable D-1). The only user-facing name is "Finger drawing" (the earlier "Pen mode" naming is removed everywhere: tool hints, view cluster, overflow, semantics, tests).
  - *Given* a device on which no stylus has EVER been detected (the `StylusSeen` flag is stored per device and set by the first `PointerKind.Stylus` event), *When* the user starts a one-finger drag with a drawing tool, *Then* the default is **Finger drawing ON** (finger draws), because a finger-only phone must be usable without finding a hidden setting; the hint "Finger drawing is on. Drag to draw; use two fingers to move the view. [Turn off] [Got it]" (item `FingerHintOn`, recurrence class twice-or-acted per the table of Task 3.6.7c: shown on the first trigger, once more on the next trigger unless an action button was pressed, then retired) is announced politely, and the setting appears in the view cluster and overflow.
  - *Given* a device that has detected a stylus (`StylusSeen` true) and the user has not changed the setting, *Then* the default flips to OFF (finger pans, stylus draws), the hint "Finger drags move the view. Turn on Finger drawing to draw with touch. [Turn on] [Got it]" (item `FingerHintOff`, twice-or-acted, same rule) shows on the first one-finger drag with a drawing tool, and an explicit user choice is stored and never overridden by `StylusSeen`.
  - *Given* Finger drawing ON, *Then* two-finger gestures still pinch-zoom and pan, a palm touch during an active stylus stroke is ignored, and a second pointer during a one-finger draw cancels the preview (Story 3.1.1); *Given* a finger-only phone with Finger drawing ON, *Then* every drawing tool is usable with one finger and Crosshair (non-drag) placement remains available.
  - *Given* Finger drawing OFF, *When* the user selects a drawing tool, *Then* the tool hint reads "Drag with a pen or mouse. With touch, turn on Finger drawing, or use Crosshair." and the toggle is reachable in at most 2 steps.
- The ON-to-OFF flip is never silent (UX-102; D-9). *Given* Finger drawing is at its default ON (no explicit user choice) and the first `PointerKind.Stylus` event of the device completes (pen-up of that stroke, never mid-gesture), *Then* `StylusSeen` becomes true, the effective setting flips to OFF, and the `PenDetected` banner candidate (Warning; ux.md banner table: "Pen found. Finger off.", primary [Back on], Details and Got it under More; its Details sheet carries "A pen was detected, so Finger drawing is now off. One finger moves the view; the pen draws." with [Turn Finger drawing back on]) shows through the `SketchNoticeQueue`, announced politely, once per device; *Given* the user had explicitly chosen ON or OFF, *Then* no flip and no notice occur.
- One-finger pan has stated alternatives while Finger drawing is ON (UX-102; A5). *Given* Finger drawing ON and a drawing tool active, *Then* one-finger pan is unavailable by design and the view moves by: (a) a two-finger drag (always), (b) the Hand tool (dock, H), (c) the edge-pan strip, only on a target Task 0.1.4d marked Supported (UX-121; geometry in the system-gesture criterion below): a one-finger drag that STARTS in the strip pans instead of drawing, (d) with the Select tool active a one-finger drag on empty canvas pans (marquee is by pen, mouse or the Crosshair marquee), and (e) the pan arrows of Task 3.6.3d (UX-120), shown by default (Auto) only while Finger drawing is ON and a touch or pen pointer is present (collapsed to one 44 dp "Pan" button that opens the pad), so the safe default still leaves four ways to pan (two fingers, the Hand tool, the Select-tool pan and the arrows) and a mouse- or keyboard-only desktop does not show the pad unless "Pan arrows: Always" is chosen. *Given* Finger drawing ON on a Supported target and a strip whose inset line is at x = 24 dp, *Then* a one-finger drag starting at x = 60 dp (inside the 44 dp strip, inside the protected span) pans and creates nothing; starting at x = 80 dp it draws; starting at x = 60 dp but outside the protected span it draws; starting at x = 10 dp (in the system inset) is not hit-tested by the app. Task 5 of Story 6.2.8 checks that users find a way to pan.
- Stylus eraser end and barrel button (UX-101; D-10). *Given* a stylus event with `PointerKind.Eraser` (eraser end), *When* the stroke starts, *Then* the Eraser tool is active for that stroke only and the previous tool is restored at pen-up (one undo step per erase stroke); *Given* a barrel button pressed during a stylus stroke, *Then* the stroke is the secondary click (context menu at the pen position, same as a right click); *Given* a target where Spike 0.1.4 finds the tool type or button state unavailable (for example a wasmJs `pointerType=pen` event without button data), *Then* the mapping is absent there and the Eraser tool stays reachable from the strip; scripted with synthesised `MotionEvent` tool types in `PointerAdapterTest`.
- Pinch zoom and wheel zoom preserve the focal point; fit-to-content and zoom range `SceneLimits.minZoomPercent` to `maxZoomPercent` (1%-3000%).
- The Finger drawing state is always visible (UX-80, UX-102; D-9, D-43). *Given* any layout or width, *Then* the TOP BAR (never scrolls, never collapses; ux.md chrome table `topbar`) carries a persistent chip labelled "Finger: On" or "Finger: Off" ("On" or "Off" from font scale 1.5; text plus icon, never colour alone; semantics "Finger drawing, on, switch"; the view cluster offers the same toggle with the full label), so it is on screen even when the tool strip scrolls; activating it toggles the setting and it is not hidden in the overflow menu at any width; the chip composable belongs to Task 3.1.4d (`TopBar.kt`) and `TopBarTest` asserts it sits in the top bar and not in the strip; `FingerDrawingDefaultsTest` asserts only the setting and its state text.
- Palm rejection on touch laptops and tablets (UX-102; Spike 0.1.4, Task 3.1.2h). *Given* a device with touch and no stylus (a touch laptop or tablet) and Finger drawing ON, *Then* the adapter ignores a touch the platform classifies as a palm (`TOOL_TYPE_PALM` or an `ACTION_CANCEL` palm cancel on Android; no app-defined size threshold exists) and every touch that arrives while a mouse or pen stroke is active; the app implements no other palm detection, relies on the OS or driver where it rejects palms, and the help page says so: where neither rejects, the user turns Finger drawing off or uses the Hand tool.
- The edge-pan strip has ONE geometry, a protected span and a proactive safe default (UX-102, UX-121; D-18, D-32; Task 0.1.4d measures it). *Given* Finger drawing ON, *Then* the strip starts at the system gesture inset (`WindowInsets.systemGestures`; 0 where there is no inset), is 44 dp wide (24 dp visible band next to the inset, meeting 3:1 non-text contrast in light, dark and high-contrast, plus 20 dp hit-only; this replaces the earlier "outer 24 dp" and "inside the inset" statements, which contradicted each other and the 44 dp touch floor), and covers ONLY a protected span: on Android the span is at most the height the platform grants per edge (200 dp expected, the height recorded by Task 0.1.4d), vertically centred on its edge, registered with `View.setSystemGestureExclusionRects` for exactly that span and drawn and hit-tested only there; outside the span a drag draws. On iOS the host view controller defers screen-edge gestures (`preferredScreenEdgesDeferringSystemGestures`, compile-only in v1) and on web the canvas host sets `touch-action: none` and `overscroll-behavior: none`; the iOS and Safari defaults are OFF (device behaviour UNVERIFIED).
  - *Safe default*: `EdgePanPolicy.defaultSide(target)` returns Off for every target Task 0.1.4d did not record Supported, for Android below API 29, and for any device that cannot request exclusion, and Both where Supported; with Off the strip is not drawn or hit-tested, the Hand tool is pinned in the compact dock and the pan arrows keep their own Auto rule (UX-120). *Given* a spike result of NOT Supported (or no result), *Then* a unit test shows `defaultSide` is Off, the strip composable is absent from the semantics tree, and the three other pan paths pass `PointerAdapterTest`.
  - *Reactive backstop (secondary, only where Supported)*: *Given* the system cancels a one-finger drag that starts in the strip twice in one session, *Then* the strip hides for the session, the device is persistently marked `ExclusionFailed` (so the NEXT session starts Off through `defaultSide`), and a one-time notice through the `OnboardingSequencer` (item `EdgePanStopped`) says "Edge pan stopped working here. Use two fingers, the Hand tool or the pan arrows.". The setting "Edge pan side: Both / Left / Right / Off" lets the user choose, with ONE default rule: where Supported the default is Right on Compact width (two 44 dp strips would cost 88 of 360 dp) and Both from 600 dp, Off elsewhere (no dominant-hand rule); two-finger pan, the Hand tool and the pan arrows never depend on the strip.
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-07 finger drag pans with Finger drawing OFF and draws when ON; UX-09 wheel and pinch zoom keep the focal point, Fit to content in one step, zoom clamped 1%-3000%.
**Files**: `UI2/SketchPointerAdapter.kt`, `UI2/EdgePanStrip.kt` (new), `UI2/EdgePanPolicy.kt` (new), `RT/settings/EdgePanSettings.kt` (new), `CM/platform/SketchPlatformInput.kt` (new; seams), `kmp/src/androidMain/kotlin/dev/stapler/stelekit/platform/AndroidSketchPlatformInput.kt` (new), `kmp/src/iosMain/kotlin/dev/stapler/stelekit/platform/IosSketchPlatformInput.kt` (new), `kmp/src/wasmJsMain/kotlin/dev/stapler/stelekit/platform/WebSketchPlatformInput.kt` (new), `CM/sketch/ViewTransform.kt` (extend), `AT/sketch/v2/PointerAdapterTest.kt`, `JT/sketch/v2/PointerAdapterDesktopTest.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/FingerDrawingFlipTest.kt` (new; UX-102), `AT/sketch/v2/EdgePanStripTest.kt` (new; UX-102, D-18), `AT/sketch/v2/PlatformEdgeHostsTest.kt` (new; UX-102)
##### Task 3.1.2a: Adapter normalising to `InputEvent` per Spike 0.1.4 findings (M)
##### Task 3.1.2b: Pan/zoom/hand tool, wheel units, fit-to-content (S)
##### Task 3.1.2c: Robolectric + desktop tests; wasm `touch-action` host (S)
##### Task 3.1.2d: `FingerDrawing` setting store, `StylusSeen` flag, one-time hints shown through the `OnboardingSequencer`, rename sweep of "Pen mode" with `FingerDrawingDefaultsTest` (S)
##### Task 3.1.2e: Finger-drawing flip notice at first stylus pen-up and Select-tool one-finger pan, with `FingerDrawingFlipTest`; it exposes only the `FingerDrawing` state, setting and flip notice, and the chip that shows them is Task 3.1.4d (S)
##### Task 3.1.2f: Stylus eraser-end and barrel-button mapping per Spike 0.1.4 availability (XS)
##### Task 3.1.2g: Edge-strip policy, geometry and setting: `EdgePanPolicy` (safe default Off unless Task 0.1.4d recorded Supported; default side Right on Compact width, Both from 600 dp), the strip composable (inset start, 44 dp hit, 24 dp visible band, protected span) with its hit routing in the pointer adapter, the persisted `ExclusionFailed` marker and "Edge pan side" setting, the cancel-detection backstop that raises the `EdgePanStopped` onboarding item, `EdgePanStripTest` (M); needs Task 3.1.2h (the platform hosts), the `OnboardingSequencer` and the pan arrows of Task 3.6.3d; reclassified from S in iteration 4, split in iteration 5
##### Task 3.1.2h: Platform edge hosts and palm classification: the `EdgeGestureExclusion` and `PalmClassifier` seams, the Android exclusion host (rects for the protected span only, re-registered on every layout pass), the iOS edge-deferral hook (compile-only), the wasm `overscroll-behavior` host EXTENDING the `WebSketchPlatformInput.kt` wasm host that Task 3.1.2c creates for `touch-action` (one file, not a second host), Android palm classification (`TOOL_TYPE_PALM` and palm `ACTION_CANCEL`), `PlatformEdgeHostsTest` (M); needs Task 0.1.4d results; split from Task 3.1.2g in iteration 5

#### Story 3.1.3: Tool strip, tool lock, adaptive layout
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- The dock layout shows a bottom-docked strip and the rail layout a left rail (which one applies is decided on both axes by Story 3.1.4, not by width alone); Undo/Redo/Delete stay pinned (UX-05, UX-06); tool lock (Q) keeps the tool after one shape (UX-04).
  - *Given* a 360 x 640 dp window, *When* `ToolStrip` composes, *Then* it is bottom-docked (grouped or flat per the one rule of Story 3.1.4) and the style sheet opens as a bottom sheet; at 1280 x 800 dp it is a rail with a side pane.
- Touch target and spacing options (UX-92). *Given* the setting "Larger touch handles" (default ON on touch-primary devices), *Then* selection handles have at least a 44 dp (target 48 dp) hit area while the visible handle stays 8 dp; and *Given* the pinned dock, *Then* Delete is separated from Redo by at least 16 dp or a divider so a mis-tap on Delete next to Redo is not the default outcome (Delete also requires no confirmation but is one Undo away and announces "Deleted: <n> elements").
- Fold/unfold and rotation keep state (UX-93). *Given* a selection, an open panel tab, the active tool and an in-progress text edit, *When* the window changes between compact and expanded (fold/unfold, rotation, resize), *Then* the selection, active tool, tool lock, open panel tab, zoom/pan centre and the text overlay contents survive, and focus returns to the same logical control.
- Compact-width entry to Constrain and Geometry (UX-118; D-8). *Given* width 360 dp, *Then* the dock shows a Constrain button whenever one or more elements are selected (disabled with its reason when none is, never absent on technical or converted sheets), the overflow menu always lists "Constrain (K)" and, on technical or converted sheets, a "Geometry" flyout group (Point X, Arc C) is a dock group; when the strip is flat Point and Arc are flat tools; every item is reachable by touch with no keyboard.
- ONE touch-target floor (UX-46, UX-92; D-17). *Given* a device on which a touch or pen pointer is present (touch-primary form factor, or a touch or pen event has been observed), *Then* every interactive control, selection-handle hit area, constraint-glyph hit area and flyout target (the separate chevron target of a split-button group, whose main target is a second 44 dp target; the pair's size is in the chrome table) has a hit area of at least 44 dp (primary dock buttons at least 48 dp); *Given* a mouse-only device, *Then* the floor is 24 dp (WCAG 2.5.8 minimum); the visible size of a handle (8 dp) or glyph may stay smaller than its hit area, and `UxA11yAcceptanceTest` names any node below the floor for the active pointer class. This replaces the earlier mixed 24 dp / 44 dp wording; there is no other floor.
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-05 Undo, Redo and Delete operable in every layout; UX-06 bottom dock at 360 dp, left rail at 840 dp, no control overlaps the crosshair chip, zoom cluster or shell mic button.
**Files**: `UI2/ToolStrip.kt`, `UI2/SketchScreenV2.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/ToolStripTest.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/AdaptiveStateSurvivalTest.kt` (new; UX-92, UX-93)
##### Task 3.1.3a: Strip + split-button group flyouts (main target plus separate flyout target) + tool lock state + the compact-width Constrain and Geometry entries (M)
##### Task 3.1.3b: Adaptive host, crosshair chip collision fix, zoom/grid/snap toggles (S)
##### Task 3.1.3c: Larger-touch-handles option (the `TouchTargetFloor`), Delete/Redo spacing, fold/unfold and rotation state survival (S)

#### Story 3.1.4: Size classes on both axes and the chrome budget (UX-119, UX-126, UX-128, UX-88; needs 3.1.3 and 3.6.4b)
**As a** user on a landscape phone, a small split-screen or a large-text setting, **I want** the editor to keep a usable canvas and every essential control, **so that** layout is not decided by width alone and chrome never eats the drawing area.
**Single source.** Every layout figure (size classes, row heights, extents, minimum canvas, banner text budget, pad variants, toast lane) is defined ONCE, in the chrome table of ux.md section 1, and computed by `check-plan.py`; this story states rules and cites the table, and no figure is restated here (the script fails on a stale one).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Layout is chosen on width AND height (UX-119). *Given* each window of the table, *When* `ChromeBudget` picks a layout, *Then* it equals the table's computed layout column: compact height uses the LEFT RAIL and a SIDE sheet whatever the width (no bottom dock, no bottom sheet), Expanded width at regular height uses the rail and the persistent pane, everything else the dock and the bottom sheet (Medium width has no layout of its own); the rail is the same fixed width whether tools are flat or grouped (a split button's targets lie along the strip axis), and the side sheet falls back to a full-width overlay with [Close] where the room is under its minimum.
- A minimum canvas holds with all chrome counted, in the worst case (UX-119). *Given* every GUARANTEED window of the table at font scales 1.0 and 2.0 and a Blocking banner showing, *Then* the canvas is at least the table's minimum tall and wide, rows collapse only until that holds and strictly in the table's order (the compact-height rail keeps the hint/status row and the selection bar permanently collapsed), and they return in reverse order; the top bar and the dock never collapse. The expected canvas heights are the computed column of the table, asserted by `ChromeBudgetTest` and `ChromeBudgetPolicyTest` from the generated chrome-table fixture of Task 3.6.4c (no number is typed into any test).
- Degraded layout below the guarantee (UX-128; WCAG 1.4.10). *Given* the table's degraded windows (a web page at 400% zoom), *Then* the same rules apply with no minimum claimed, nothing clips, there is no page-level horizontal scroll, panels open as full-width overlays with [Close], and every control stays reachable by a scroll path.
- One banner slot, ONE order, and ownership (UX-119, UX-126; engineering E2). *Given* all candidates of the banner table pending at once (their count is computed from the table; a producer story emits each, see Story 3.6.7), *Then* exactly one occupies the slot, chosen by the table's rank, the rest wait in the `SketchNoticeQueue` and the diagnostics list, the slot does not grow, and when the occupant is dismissed or acted on the next rank appears. `BannerSlot` ONLY renders the head of the queue: it imports no producer, and producers import no `BannerSlot` (they emit `BannerCandidate` entries through `DiagnosticsPort`, Story 3.6.4a).
- Banner content fits (UX-126). *Given* each row of the banner table at 320 and 360 dp wide and font scales 1.0 and 2.0, *Then* it renders as one row (severity icon, summary or, when the summary exceeds the text budget at that width and scale, the short title, one primary button, one More button), at most two lines, buttons at the floor, nothing clipped, inside the slot height; Blocking rows have no Dismiss; every summary and short title is word-wrapped greedily at the computed line width (a word breaks only after a hyphen) and must fit the row's two lines, which `check-plan.py` checks and `ChromeBudgetTest` repeats on the rendered node; a row whose short title is "-" (the consent row `UpgradeChip`) is replaced by a one-time sheet that shows its consequence in full where the summary does not fit, never by a short title; Details is the first item of More (a row whose primary is "Learn more" opens it and does not repeat it); every primary label is 1-2 words, its accessible name is the label plus its object and contains the visible label (WCAG 2.5.3, 2.4.6, 4.1.2), names are unique and no visible label is reused for a different meaning, and the live region announces the FULL summary at every font scale (`bannerNames_should_ContainTheVisibleLabel_AndBeUnique`, `liveRegion_should_AnnounceFullSummary_AtFontScale2`). Dynamic text never enters a row: the `BannerCandidate` constructor (Task 3.6.4a) takes a row id and a Details string, so the pen-detected explanation and "Parallel conflicts with Horizontal on Line 3" live in Details and the diagnostics list, and the row shows the table's fixed summary.
- Touch-required controls are never in a collapsible row (UX-126). *Given* a touch user with Line active, a modifier toggle needed, a slow solve or an armed picker, *Then* the chips (the modifier toggles, [Done], [Cancel], the smart-dimension chip, the Crosshair chip) are in the action chip row, a canvas overlay at the floor that exists in every window including the compact-height rail, never collapses with a row and scrolls with a chevron when it overflows; the hint/status row is text only (`ChromeBudgetTest` finishes a polyline and toggles Square on touch at the compact-height rail).
- Toast lane (UX-128; WCAG 2.4.11; Task 3.1.4c). *Given* the maximum number of toasts, *Then* they stack from the top-start corner under the banner slot, each at most the table's toast height, at most the table's share of the canvas (at least one visible), the rest as "+N more"; a toast never takes focus on arrival, Esc dismisses the newest (row 12 of the one Esc order), every toast has Dismiss as the last item of its More menu (a pointer and touch way out; `check-plan.py` fails on a row without it) and in a lane of one a newer toast moves the displayed one to the diagnostics list (`ActionChipRowTest` asserts both), and moving focus to a control a toast covers moves that toast to the diagnostics list; the audit visits every focusable node with the maximum toasts showing and finds none fully covered. Toast content fits (U7): *Given* every row of the `toast` table at 320 and 360 dp and font scales 1.0 and 2.0, *Then* it renders as one row inside `toastRow` (`toastRow2` from scale 1.5) with text that word-wraps inside two lines (placeholders expanded to `dynMax` characters, `{n:3}` to three), at most one visible action and a More button for the other actions and Details (Layer locked shows [Unlock] and More with Switch layer, Details and Dismiss), `toastCount_should_EqualTheTableColumn`, and every toast action is reachable from the keyboard through the notice region and the Notices list.
- Top bar at every font scale (U3; UX-80, UX-119). *Given* each window of the table, *Then* the top bar (Back, name, Finger drawing chip, save status, Selection menu where it exists, Overflow) never wraps, keeps the `TopBar` row height, shows the Finger drawing state as TEXT ("Finger: On/Off" below font scale 1.5, "On/Off" from 1.5; a long-press or hover tooltip carries the full label) and the save status as text below 1.5 and as a glyph-only 44 dp button from 1.5 with the full state in semantics, tooltip and live region (a failed save is also the Blocking banner), uses the short forms first and then moves the Selection menu into Overflow when the name would drop under `NameMin`, and the table's "Top bar" column is the expected result at every window (Task 3.1.4d).
- Selection menu in the compact-height rail (U5; UX-127). *Given* the rail layout at compact height, where the hint/status row and the selection bar are permanently collapsed, *Then* the top bar carries the Selection menu (a 48 dp button; label in semantics names the selection count) whose menu holds Duplicate, Group, Ungroup, Align, Order, Transform and Lock or Unlock, plus "Bind end..." when one arrow is selected (so the non-drag binding picker of Story 3.6.3 stays reachable where the selection bar is collapsed), each at the touch floor and with its shortcut, Transform holds Resize, Rotate, Move and Flip and is the handle fallback of small selections, and in the degraded 320 x 256 window the same menu is an item of Overflow.
- Overlay horizontal budget (U6; UX-119, UX-120, UX-121). *Given* each guaranteed window of the table, *Then* the mic button, the action chip row (at least `chipMin` visible), the view cluster (ONE row of seven 44 dp targets where the budget allows, otherwise TWO rows) and the edge-strip insets (`gestureInset` plus 44 dp per active side) fit as the table's "View cluster, chip row, overlay height" and "Drawing width" columns say, the chip row sits above the cluster where it cannot sit beside it, the overlays cover at most `overlayMaxPct` % of the collapsed canvas, the drawing width stays at least `minCanvasWidth`, and the pan pad has no corner cells (`ChromeBudgetTest`, `overlays_should_NeverOverlapOrExceedTheTableShare_AtEveryWindow`).
- All controls remain reachable (UX-119). *Given* every window of the table, *Then* the pinned Undo, Redo and Delete are fully visible and never scroll; the Finger drawing chip is in the top bar (never in the strip) and the top bar leaves the name its table minimum at 320 dp; every other control scrolls along the strip axis with edge fades and a visible chevron while more exist AND is also in the overflow menu; all 14 tools are reachable (flat, scrolled or from overflow); at 320 dp wide at least three non-pinned targets are visible beside the pinned trio (computed); flat versus grouped is ONE rule at every width class (`flatExtent` against the strip extent; the table's tools column says which windows are flat); the strip is laid out at the table's sizes in every pointer class, so the touch-floor switch never moves a control.
- Text scale does not clip (UX-88). *Given* font scale 2.0 at 360 dp wide, *Then* text wraps (banners to at most two lines, the top-bar name truncates with an ellipsis and the full name is in semantics), panels scroll, icons scale at most 1.3x, and no node is clipped without a scroll path (a semantics audit names any node whose bounds exceed its parent without a scrollable ancestor).
- Font scales above 2.0 (iOS accessibility sizes; UX-88). *Given* a system font scale of 3.0, *Then* the chrome text (top bar, banner, toast, strip labels) is held at 2.0 so rows keep their height, panels, sheets, dialogs and the consent sheet follow the system scale and scroll, the live region announces the full text, and the no-clipped-node audit finds nothing clipped without a scroll path (`ChromeBudgetTest.fontScale3_should_ScrollPanelsAndHoldChromeAt2`).
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-119 width-and-height layout, minimum canvas, at most three transient rows with one banner slot, pinned trio, fixed collapse order, flat-only-when-it-fits tools; UX-126 action chip row and banner content fit; UX-128 degraded layout and the toast lane.
**Files**: `UI2/ChromeBudget.kt` (new; pure policy), `UI2/BannerSlot.kt` (new; renders the head of the queue only), `UI2/ActionChipRow.kt` (new), `UI2/ToastLane.kt` (new), `UI2/TopBar.kt` (new; U3), `UI2/SelectionMenu.kt` (new; U5), `UI2/SketchScreenV2.kt`, `UI2/ToolStrip.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/ChromeBudgetPolicyTest.kt` (new), `AT/sketch/v2/ChromeBudgetTest.kt` (new; UX-119, UX-126, UX-88), `AT/sketch/v2/ActionChipRowTest.kt` (new; UX-126, UX-128), `AT/sketch/v2/TopBarTest.kt` (new; U3, U5), `kmp/src/commonTest/kotlin/BUILD.bazel` and `BT/AllBusinessTests.kt` (Task 3.1.4e registers `ChromeBudgetPolicyTest`)

##### Task 3.1.4b: Apply the policy in the adaptive host (rail and side sheet at compact height, the `BannerSlot` host rendering the head of the `SketchNoticeQueue`, collapsing hint/status row, banner badge and selection menu, scrolling strip with chevron and overflow) and `ChromeBudgetTest` at every window of the table with every banner candidate emitted into the real queue by a fake producer (M)

### Epic 3.2: Shapes, selection, eraser, frame tool
#### Story 3.2.1: Rectangle, diamond, ellipse, line/polyline tools
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Shift constrains.
  - *Given* Rectangle tool, *When* drag from (0,0) to (100,60) with Shift, *Then* a square of side 100 is committed; From centre is a toggle chip in the action chip row (and Alt on Windows and macOS only, never on Linux; no function is Alt-only, UX-109); line with Shift snaps to 15 degree steps.
- Multi-point line finish.
  - *Given* Line tool, *When* click (0,0), (40,0), (40,40) then double-click, *Then* one `LineElement` with 3 points is committed.
**Files**: `V2/tools/ShapeTools.kt`, `V2/tools/LineTool.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/tools/ShapeToolsTest.kt`
##### Task 3.2.1a: Shape tools with modifiers (S)
##### Task 3.2.1b: Line/polyline tool + point editing mode (M)

#### Story 3.2.2: Selection, marquee, transform, rotate, multi-select
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Resize keeps rotation; group transform.
  - *Given* two elements selected with bounds (0,0,40,40) and (60,0,100,40), *When* the right handle is dragged 100 units, *Then* both scale about the left edge and `undo` restores both in one step.
- ONE selectability rule (UX-12; ux.md section 4). The canvas (pointer, marquee, Select all, Tab traversal) never selects a locked element or an element on a hidden layer; the Elements list and Go to element MAY select a locked element (it shows [Unlock] and can be renamed) but never a hidden one (its row offers [Show layer]); Lock leaves the just-locked elements selected until the selection changes; attempting to edit a locked layer shows the `LayerLocked` toast "Locked layer: Walls." with Unlock (Switch layer and Details under More); a mixed selection applies to unlocked elements only and shows the `LockedSkipped` toast "Locked items skipped." with Unlock all (the count is in its Details).
- Handles never overlap (UX-127; ux.md section 1, computed tiers). *Given* a selection whose shorter side on screen is at or above the full tier (three touch floors), *Then* eight resize handles and the rotation handle show; at or above the corner tier (two floors) and below the full tier, four corner handles and the rotation handle; below that, none, the outline shows, the status line says "Zoom in to use handles" and Resize, Rotate and Move remain available from the selection bar's Transform menu, the Stats fields and Crosshair mode; the tiers use the active pointer class's floor but the strip layout is unaffected; `HandleLayoutTest` generates sizes for both floors and asserts that no two shown hit areas overlap.
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-10 resizing a multi-selection by one handle is one undo step.
**Files**: `V2/tools/SelectTool.kt`, `V2/tools/TransformMath.kt`, `UI2/SelectionOverlay.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/tools/SelectToolTest.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/HandleLayoutTest.kt` (new; UX-127)
##### Task 3.2.2a: Select/marquee/shift-add/duplicate (Ctrl+D and the selection-bar button; Alt+drag only on Windows and macOS, native or web, never on Linux) (M)
##### Task 3.2.2b: Handles, rotate, flip, multi-element transform math (M)
##### Task 3.2.2c: Selection overlay (dynamic layer) + locked feedback (S)
##### Task 3.2.2d: Handle tiers by on-screen selection size, the selection bar's Transform menu and `HandleLayoutTest` (S)

#### Story 3.2.3: Eraser, hand, frame tools
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Eraser removes whole elements touched by the stroke in one undo step; frame tool creates a `FrameElement` and auto-adopts elements dropped fully inside.
  - *Given* Eraser stroke crossing `r1` and `r2`, *Then* both deleted in one transaction; *Given* a frame drawn around `r3`, *Then* `r3.frameId == frame.id`; dragging `r3` out clears it.
- Eraser preview, touch and non-drag paths (UX-101). *Given* the Eraser tool and a drag across `r1` and `r2`, *Then* while the pointer is down every element the path currently touches is PREVIEWED as erased (drawn at 30% opacity with a dashed outline, so the cue is neither colour nor opacity alone) and nothing is deleted until release; release deletes all previewed elements in ONE transaction; Esc or a second pointer cancels with no command and restores them; locked and hidden-layer elements are never previewed and the status reads "2 locked elements were not erased" when the path crossed some. *Given* a touch pointer with Finger drawing OFF, *Then* a TAP on an element with the Eraser tool erases that one element (a tap is not a drag, so it works while one-finger drags pan), announced "Erased: Rectangle 2" with Undo; with Finger drawing ON a one-finger drag erases like a stylus. *Given* the non-drag path, *Then* either the elements are selected and Delete is pressed, or in Crosshair mode the user chooses Erase, places the crosshair on an element and Apply erases the element under it (ties go to the topmost), each one undo step.
**Files**: `V2/tools/EraserTool.kt`, `V2/tools/FrameTool.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/tools/EraserFrameToolTest.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/EraserTouchTest.kt` (new; UX-101)
##### Task 3.2.3a: Eraser (S)
##### Task 3.2.3b: Frame tool + auto-membership (S)
##### Task 3.2.3c: Eraser preview, tap-to-erase on touch, Crosshair erase, locked-skip status (S)

#### Story 3.2.4: Point and Arc tools (technical geometry; OD-8, needs 3.1.1, 1.2.1, 1.3.3)
**As a** user sketching constrained geometry, **I want** to place free points and three-point arcs, **so that** constraints (coincident, point-on-object, tangent to an arc) have geometry to attach to.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Point tool.
  - *Given* the Point tool, *When* the user clicks (40, 40) (or places it with the crosshair, no drag), *Then* one `PointElement` at (40, 40) is committed in one undo step; a click that snaps to a line endpoint adds a Coincident in the same transaction (Story 4.2.2).
- Arc tool.
  - *Given* the Arc tool, *When* start (0,0), end (100,0) and a bulge point (50,-30) are placed, *Then* one `ArcElement` through those three points is committed with a positive radius and the end angle after the start angle; collinear points commit nothing and show "Nothing drawn"; Esc mid-way cancels with no command.
- Both are keyboard-reachable and have a non-drag path (clicks and the crosshair pad only).
**Files**: `V2/tools/PointTool.kt`, `V2/tools/ArcTool.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/tools/PointArcToolsTest.kt`
##### Task 3.2.4a: Point tool and `ArcFromThreePoints` math (XS)
##### Task 3.2.4b: Arc tool state machine, hint text, registry entry (S)

### Epic 3.3: Arrows and binding
#### Story 3.3.1: Straight and curved arrows with arrowheads
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Three-point curved arrow.
  - *Given* Arrow tool with Round type, *When* points (0,0), (50,30), (100,0) are placed, *Then* one `ArrowElement` with 3 points and `roundness != null` is committed with `endArrowhead=Arrow`.
**Files**: `V2/tools/ArrowTool.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/tools/ArrowToolTest.kt`
##### Task 3.3.1a: Arrow tool (S)
##### Task 3.3.1b: Arrow type/arrowhead style fields + elbow stored-only (import keeps `elbowed`, renders as polyline; a visible gap against the Excalidraw baseline, accepted via OD-7) (S)

#### Story 3.3.2: Binding, rebinding, unbinding
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Arrow follows moved shape.
  - *Given* `ArrowElement a1` end-bound (Orbit, fixedPoint (1.0, 0.5)) to `RectElement r1` at (0,0,100,50), *When* `r1` moves by (200, 80), *Then* the arrow end is at (300, 105) within 1e-6 and the move is one undo step.
- Ctrl (Cmd on macOS) disables binding on drop (not Alt); binding highlight target within snap radius; bound to both ends of the same shape is allowed; rotated target uses the local frame.
- Binding invariant property: after any command sequence every `Binding.elementId` resolves to a live element.
- Binding abort/pivot (requirements; Task 6.2.9b). *Given* `BindingInvariantPropertyTest` cannot hold the invariant over rotate, group transform and paste, *Then* the engine ships bind-on-drop, follow-on-move and unbind-on-delete only: rotate and group transform leave a bound arrow end at its last position, unbind it and show a notice; elbow routing stays stored-only (OD-7); a dangling binding after `reconcile` blocks release.
**Files**: `V2/binding/BindingEngine.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/BindingEngineTest.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/BindingInvariantPropertyTest.kt`
##### Task 3.3.2a: Bind-on-drop + endpoint recompute on move/resize/rotate (M)
##### Task 3.3.2b: Copy/paste/duplicate matrix (arrow without target unbinds; both in set rebinds) (M)
##### Task 3.3.2c: Property test (S)

#### Story 3.3.3: Text in shapes and arrow labels
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Container grows to fit; deleting the container deletes its text.
  - *Given* `RectElement r1` 80x40 with `TextElement t1(containerId=r1)` text "Authentication Service" at 20 px, *When* the text is committed, *Then* `r1` height becomes the wrapped text height plus padding and deleting `r1` also deletes `t1` in one step.
**Files**: `V2/commands/ContainerTextCommands.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/ContainerTextTest.kt`

##### Task 3.3.3b: Arrow label placement at midpoint (S)

### Epic 3.4: Text, freedraw, image
#### Story 3.4.1: Text tool and in-place editor
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Empty text auto-deletes on blur; multi-line IME editing via `BasicTextField` overlay positioned at the transformed rect (works at zoom 2.5).
  - *Given* Text tool, *When* click at (10,10), type "Hi", Enter, "There", blur, *Then* a `TextElement` with originalText "Hi\nThere" exists; blurring with empty text leaves nothing.
- Touch commit and hint text (UX-124). *Given* the text overlay opened on touch with no hardware keyboard, *Then* the mini bar shows a [Done] button, the hint reads "Tap Done or tap outside to finish" and mentions no key, tapping outside the overlay commits the text, [Done] commits exactly like Esc (an empty text is removed as before), and *Given* a hardware keyboard attached, *Then* the keyboard hint "Esc to finish" is shown instead.
- Over-long text is refused, never truncated (UX-23).
  - *Given* an open text overlay with "Hello", *When* 10,001 characters are pasted (more than `SceneLimits.maxTextChars`), *Then* the paste is refused, a dialog states "Text too long (max 10,000 characters)" with OK, and the existing text is unchanged; typing past the limit is refused the same way.
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-20 multi-line IME text at zoom 2.5 with the overlay above the soft keyboard; UX-21 blurring an empty text element leaves nothing and shows no error; UX-22 shortcut letters type into the overlay and never switch tools.
**Files**: `V2/tools/TextTool.kt`, `UI2/TextEditorOverlay.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/TextEditorTest.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/tools/TextToolTest.kt` (new; the pure commit-multiline and delete-when-empty cases)
##### Task 3.4.1a: Tool + commands (S)
##### Task 3.4.1b: Overlay with IME, zoom mapping, soft-keyboard inset (M)
##### Task 3.4.1c: Paste/typing limit against `SceneLimits.maxTextChars` with the refusal dialog (XS)

#### Story 3.4.2: Freedraw tool
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Mouse works without pressure; stylus pressure stored.
  - *Given* Stylus events with pressures [0.2, 0.6, 0.9], *When* committed, *Then* `pressures` has 3 entries and `simulatePressure=false`; with mouse events `simulatePressure=true`.
**Files**: `V2/tools/FreedrawTool.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/tools/FreedrawToolTest.kt`
##### Task 3.4.2a: Tool + commit-time simplification (S)

#### Story 3.4.3: Image tool and asset store
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Images by hash, bounded.
  - *Given* a 1.2 MB PNG picked, *When* placed, *Then* the bytes are stored once under the graph assets by SHA-256 `fileId`, the element holds `fileId` not base64, and a file over `SceneLimits.maxImageBytes` (60 MB) is refused with a stated cap; header dimensions are read before decoding (no bitmap allocation above `SceneLimits.maxImagePixels`, 16,000,000 px).
- Referenced assets are enumerable for copy and export.
  - *Given* a scene with 2 `ImageElement`s of one `fileId` and a deleted element holding another, *When* `referencedFileIds(scene)` runs, *Then* it returns exactly the live set `{fileId1}` (consumed by Stories 5.1.3, 5.2.1 and 6.1.2).
**Files**: `RT/image/ImageAssetStore.kt`, `V2/image/ReferencedAssets.kt` (pure), `V2/tools/ImageTool.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/ImageAssetStoreTest.kt`

##### Task 3.4.3b: Tool, placeholder for missing file, header-size probe (S)

### Epic 3.5: Organisation and styling
#### Story 3.5.1: Groups, frames, z-order
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Nested groups.
  - *Given* `r1`,`r2` grouped as G1 then G1 and `r3` grouped as G2, *When* `r1` is clicked, *Then* G2 is selected; double-click descends to G1, then `r1`; Ctrl+Shift+G ungroups one level.
- Bring-to-front.
  - *Given* z-keys [a,b,c], *When* `a` is brought to front, *Then* its `ZKey` sorts after `c` and ids keep their order otherwise.
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-11 selecting a grouped element selects the outer group, double-click descends, ungroup by shortcut and menu.
**Files**: `V2/commands/GroupFrameCommands.kt` (extends 1.3.2b), `V2/tools/SelectTool.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/GroupFrameTest.kt` (new; nested-group select/descend and z-order cases)
##### Task 3.5.1a: Group selection semantics in select tool (M)
##### Task 3.5.1b: z-order commands UI wiring (S)

#### Story 3.5.2: Layers model and panel (ADR-006)
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Create, reorder, lock, hide, opacity, rename, delete with undo.
  - *Given* layers [Base, Walls], *When* "Walls" is moved up and set opacity 0.5, *Then* paint order puts Walls above Base, effective element opacity halves, and one undo reverts the move only.
- Panel hidden until a second layer exists; toggles read "Layer Walls, visible, switch" to TalkBack; Move up/Move down buttons as non-drag reorder.
- Deleting a layer that holds elements is confirmed (UX-18).
  - *Given* layer "Walls" with 12 elements, *When* Delete layer is chosen, *Then* a dialog reads "Delete layer 'Walls' and its 12 elements?" with the actions Delete layer and elements, Move elements to Base then delete, and Cancel; the first deletes the layer and its elements, the second moves the 12 elements to the default layer and then deletes the layer, each as ONE undo step; Cancel changes nothing; deleting the last remaining layer is disabled with a reason; the layer-name collision on rename shows "A layer with this name exists" and keeps editing; the number of layers is capped at `SceneLimits.maxLayers` (64).
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-13 hiding a layer holding the selection clears and announces it; UX-16 layers are created, reordered (drag and Move up/down), locked, hidden, renamed, given opacity and deleted, each one undo step; UX-17 layer toggles read as visible/hidden and locked/unlocked switches to TalkBack; UX-19 the layers panel is hidden until a second layer exists and reachable from the overflow menu.
**Files**: `UI2/LayersPanel.kt`, `V2/commands/LayerCommands.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/LayersPanelTest.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/UxOrganisationAcceptanceTest.kt` (new; UX-12, UX-18)
##### Task 3.5.2a: Panel + semantics (M)
##### Task 3.5.2b: Layer-aware selection, hide-clears-selection announcement (S)

#### Story 3.5.3: Align/distribute, copy/paste/duplicate, style copy
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Align and distribute.
  - *Given* rects at x = 0, 30, 100 (width 10), *When* "Distribute horizontally" runs, *Then* x becomes 0, 50, 100.
- Paste offsets repeated pastes by 10 units and remaps ids; paste Excalidraw JSON from the clipboard is routed to Story 5.1.4.

#### Story 3.5.4: Style panel and element stats
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Panel edits selection, else tool defaults; Stats panel edits x/y/w/h/angle numerically and reuses `DimensionField` for technical elements.
  - *Given* `r1` selected and stroke width Bold chosen, *Then* `r1.style.strokeWidth` updates in one undo step; with nothing selected the Rectangle tool default changes.
- The Excalidraw feature baseline is a counted checklist, not prose (requirements metric 1, D-22). *Given* `kmp/src/commonTest/resources/sketch/feature-baseline.txt` listing the 41 baseline ids (`FB-SP-01..15` style panel, `FB-AR-01..13` arrange and edit, `FB-AL-01..08` align and distribute, `FB-LB-01..05` library) each with the command or control id that implements it, *When* `FeatureBaselineCompletenessTest` runs, *Then* it fails unless the list has exactly 41 ids, every id resolves to a registered command or control (style ids against `StylePanel` model fields, arrange and align ids against command classes, library ids against `ElementLibrary` operations) and every id appears in a test name or scenario; adding or removing a baseline feature fails the test until requirements.md changes.
- The selection stays visible while styling (UX-125). *Given* a selected rectangle whose bounds would sit under the bottom sheet (compact) or side sheet (compact height) when the Style tab opens, *Then* the canvas pans, without zooming, so the bounds are inside the visible area above or beside the sheet; *Given* bounds taller than the visible area, *Then* the top edge stays visible and the status line says "Selection is larger than the visible area"; the pan is not an undo entry and the previous view returns when the sheet closes unless the user moved the view meanwhile (`StylePanelTest`).
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-15 every colour swatch has a text name for screen readers and tooltips; UX-125 the selected element stays visible above or beside the open sheet.
**Files**: `UI2/StylePanel.kt`, `UI2/ElementStatsPanel.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/StylePanelTest.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/FeatureBaselineCompletenessTest.kt` (new), `kmp/src/commonTest/resources/sketch/feature-baseline.txt` (new)

#### Story 3.5.5: Object snapping, guides, grid
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Snap to other elements' edges/centres with guide lines; grid; snap kinds map onto the existing `SnapEngine` kinds.
  - *Given* `r1` edges at x=100 and a dragged `r2` left edge at x=103 with snap radius 8 px at zoom 1, *When* released, *Then* `r2.x == 100` and one guide was shown; Ctrl disables snapping.
