#### Story 3.5.6: Element library
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Save selection to a local library and insert it; `.excalidrawlib` import/export round trip (items `{id,status,elements,created}`).
  - *Given* a saved library item of 3 elements, *When* inserted twice, *Then* both insertions have fresh ids; export then import yields equal element kinds/geometry; *Given* 200 items (`SceneLimits.maxLibraryItems`), *When* another is added, *Then* it is refused with "Library is full (200 items). Delete an item to add more."
- A real `.excalidrawlib` imports through per-item spans, not one giant array (engineering E1). A library file's top-level `libraryItems` array (v2; v1 `library` is an array of element arrays) is usually one span well over `maxImportSpanBytes` (2 MB), so the scanner's LIBRARY MODE (Task 5.1.1g) treats each ITEM of that array as one span of at most `maxImportSpanBytes` and never the array: an over-cap item is skipped with `ImportSpanTooLarge` in the report and the rest import; an item may hold image elements but a library file carries no binaries, so they import as placeholders; the file itself is capped at `maxImportBytes` and the STORED library at `maxLibraryBytes` (16 MB, summed over its items) as well as `maxLibraryItems` (200).
  - *Given* a 9 MB library file of 120 items of about 75 KB each, *Then* it imports with the largest decoder call at most `maxImportSpanBytes` (call-size counter, as in Story 5.1.1) and equals the whole-file oracle; *Given* an item of 2.5 MB, *Then* only it is skipped; *Given* a library at 15 MB of stored bytes, *When* an item of 2 MB is added, *Then* it is refused with "Library is full (16 MB). Delete an item to add more." (the count message "Library is full (200 items)..." applies to the item cap); *Given* an import that would exceed 200 items or 16 MB, *Then* it imports the items that fit in file order, reports the rest as skipped (`LibraryFull`) and never writes a partial item.
- Library success feedback (UX-114). *Given* Add to library or insert, *Then* the notice queue shows the `LibraryAdded` toast "Added to library." or the `LibraryInserted` toast "Inserted from library." (polite; both carry Undo; their Details say "Added 'Box group' to the library (3 of 200)" or "Inserted 3 elements"), and the grid scrolls the new item into view.
**Files**: `V2/library/ElementLibrary.kt`, `XCAL/LibraryCodec.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/ElementLibraryTest.kt`, `CT/sketch/excalidraw/LibraryImportSpanTest.kt` (new; E1)
##### Task 3.5.6a: Library store (per graph, Settings/asset-backed, bounded) (S)
##### Task 3.5.6b: `.excalidrawlib` codec reading through the scanner's library mode (Task 5.1.1g) and writing `{type, version, source, libraryItems}` (S)

#### Story 3.5.7: Element names and the element list (UX-110; needs 1.2.1, 3.5.2, 1.1.5)
**As a** user, **I want** every element to have a stable readable name ("Line 3") that I can see on the canvas and in a list, **so that** constraint messages, the screen reader and Go to element all point at something I can find.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Names are stable and never reused.
  - *Given* a new sheet, *When* the user draws a line, a rectangle, another line and a circle, deletes the first line, then draws another line, *Then* they are named "Line 1", "Rectangle 1", "Line 2", "Circle 1" and the new line is "Line 3" (per-kind counters are monotonic per sheet and a deleted ordinal is never reused, so a message never points at a different element than before); an `EllipseElement` with `rx == ry` is named "Circle n", other ellipses "Ellipse n"; the counters live in the sheet (`nameCounters`) and each element stores its `ordinal` and an optional user `name` (at most `SceneLimits.maxNameChars`); an imported scene gets ordinals assigned in z order; the Excalidraw export carries the name in `customData.stelekit.name`.
- Names are visible on the canvas.
  - *Given* an element is selected or is named in a message (constraint conflict, Go to element, Pick target), *Then* a name tag chip "Line 3" is drawn at its anchor and removed when the message is dismissed; *Given* the view toggle "Show names" (view cluster and overflow; default ON on scaled/technical and converted sheets, OFF on diagram sheets; stored per sheet), *Then* tag chips are drawn for the elements in the viewport, decluttered by the same rule as constraint glyphs (UX-106: overlapping tags collapse to a count badge) and capped by `SceneLimits.maxSemanticsNodes`.
- Names are visible in a list.
  - *Given* the side-pane tab "Elements" (always present, like Constraints; on compact it is a bottom-sheet tab), *Then* it lists layers as expandable groups containing their elements in z order, each row showing kind icon, name, text label if any, and visible/locked state as text plus icon; selecting a row selects the element and pans to it; Enter or F2 renames in place (a name already used on the sheet shows "An element with this name exists" and keeps editing; Esc reverts); the list is virtualised (rows are built only for the visible window) and rows on hidden layers are shown dimmed with "(hidden)" text.
- Names feed every other surface.
  - *Given* the element named "Line 3", *Then* its semantics label is "Line 3" plus its kind-specific detail (Story 3.6.2), constraint messages cite "Line 3" (Story 4.2.3), and `ElementLabelIndex` (Story 3.6.5) indexes both names and text.
**Files**: `V2/scene/ElementName.kt`, `V2/scene/NameCounters.kt`, `UI2/ElementsPanel.kt`, `UI2/NameTags.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/ElementNameTest.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/ElementsPanelTest.kt`
##### Task 3.5.7a: `ordinal`/`name` fields, `NameCounters`, codec and export fields, assignment in `AddElement` and import (S)
##### Task 3.5.7b: Elements tab (virtualised list, rename, select-and-pan), "Show names" tag chips and their declutter (M)

### Epic 3.6: Keyboard, accessibility, diagnostics
#### Story 3.6.1: Keyboard map and shortcuts sheet
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Excalidraw-compatible map (V,R,D,O,A,L,P,T,E,H,F, 1-9, Q, Ctrl+Z/Shift+Z, Ctrl+C/V/X/D, Ctrl+G, brackets, arrow nudge with Shift = 10 units, Delete).
  - *Given* focus on the canvas, *When* Ctrl+Z is pressed after a move, *Then* the move is undone and the live region announces "Undid: move rectangle", and an empty history announces "Nothing to undo" (UX-43); "?" opens a shortcuts sheet and Esc closes it and returns focus to the canvas (UX-42).
- Single-letter shortcuts meet WCAG 2.1.4 and are settled BEFORE the map is frozen (U4, UX-85; owner-confirmable D-4). Single-character tool shortcuts (V R D O A L P T E H F Q and the digits 1-9) are (a) active ONLY while the canvas has keyboard focus (the 2.1.4 "active only on focus" condition), AND (b) behind the `SingleKeyShortcuts` setting (Settings > Sketches and the shortcuts sheet), defaults per UX-108: ON on desktop, OFF on web, ON on Android only with a hardware keyboard and no touch exploration (TalkBack); with it OFF only modifier chords work and tool selection stays reachable from the tool strip. Modifier chords are unaffected.
  - *Given* `SingleKeyShortcuts` OFF and the canvas focused, *When* "R" is pressed, *Then* no tool changes and nothing is announced; *Given* focus in a text field, the style panel or a dialog, *Then* single letters never switch tools in either setting (UX-22).
- Non-QWERTY layouts and macOS (U4).
  - *Given* an AZERTY or Dvorak layout, *When* the key that produces the character "r" is pressed, *Then* Rectangle is chosen (tool letters match the produced character, not the physical key code); *Given* a layout where "[" or "]" needs a modifier, *Then* the physical-key fallback for send backward/forward applies and the shortcuts sheet shows the key the layout actually needs; *Given* macOS, *Then* every Ctrl chord in the map (Z, Shift+Z, C, V, X, D, G, [, ], 0, +/-) is bound to Cmd and the shortcuts sheet displays Cmd (Ctrl+Z does not fire there); Ctrl+Y redo is Windows/Linux only.
- Shortcut scheme beyond the digits 1-9 (UX-100; D-8). 14 tools: V or 1 Select, R or 2 Rectangle, D or 3 Diamond, O or 4 Ellipse, A or 5 Arrow, L or 6 Line, P or 7 Freedraw, T or 8 Text, 9 Image, H Hand, F Frame, E or 0 Eraser, X Point, C Arc. The digits 1-9 and 0 are the Excalidraw-compatible tool digits (the same order as Excalidraw's, re-checked against its live shortcuts dialog by Task 0.1.5d, U8), so every tool after the ninth has a letter; Q is tool lock.
  - *Given* `KeymapSpec`, *Then* `KeymapSpecTest` asserts every one of the 14 tool ids has at least one binding, no two tools share a binding, and the shortcuts sheet lists all 14 with their group.
- Keyboard panning (UX-105; WCAG 2.1.1). *Given* the canvas has focus and nothing is selected (or the Hand tool is active), *When* an arrow key is pressed, *Then* the view pans 40 dp per press (Shift 200 dp; PageUp/PageDown one screen height; Home fit to content), announced only as the settled zoom and centre; *Given* a selection, *Then* arrow keys nudge the selection (Shift = 10 units) as before and Ctrl+arrow (Cmd on macOS) pans regardless of selection; the shortcuts sheet lists all of them.
- Constraint shortcuts are in the core keymap (UX-111; D-8). *Given* the canvas has focus and a selection, *When* `K` (single-key setting On) or Ctrl+Shift+K (always; Ctrl+K on the web, because Ctrl+Shift+K opens the Firefox web console) is pressed, *Then* the Constrain menu opens with focus on its first enabled item, and the menu mnemonics C Coincident, H Horizontal, V Vertical, L Parallel, P Perpendicular, E Equal, T Tangent, S Symmetric, F Fixed, D Distance, A Angle, R Radius/Diameter, X Reference toggle work only while the menu is open (menu navigation, not canvas single-key shortcuts); Delete on a focused constraint row or glyph removes it; Enter or F2 on a focused dimension row or glyph edits its value (Story 4.2.7).
- Single-key shortcuts and screen readers (UX-108; D-10). The canvas exposes an application role so NVDA and JAWS switch to focus/application mode and pass keys through; with `SingleKeyShortcuts` Off on web by default a one-time hint chip reads "Single-key tool shortcuts are off. [Turn on] [Got it]" and every tool stays reachable by Tab, Enter and arrow keys in the strip. Collisions to document in the shortcuts sheet (INFERRED from screen-reader documentation, UNVERIFIED against the owner's reader): in browse mode the quick-navigation letters H (heading), F (form field), E (edit field), T (table), L (list), D (landmark) are reserved by NVDA and JAWS and coincide with our H, F, E, T, L, D tool letters, which is why the default on web is Off and the tests run in focus mode only.

- Keyboard users can skip the canvas elements (UX-117; WCAG 2.4.1). *Given* the canvas has focus and the scene holds elements, *Then* F6 and Shift+F6 move focus to the next and previous region of the ONE tab order of ux.md section 10 (top bar, notice region while it has an occupant, tool strip, canvas, selection bar where it exists, view cluster, side panel, status line) without cycling through elements, so banner and toast actions are reachable by keyboard; a link "Skip canvas elements" that is visible on focus is the first Tab stop inside the canvas region and moves focus to the next stop of that order (the action chip row, the crosshair pad or the selection bar, so Align, Order, Lock and Transform are one Tab press away, or the view cluster where none exists; the view cluster is ONE stop with roving focus over zoom out, the zoom menu, zoom in, Grid, Snap, Finger drawing and Pan, in every layout; Grid and Snap also have Ctrl+' and Alt+S chords and checkable overflow entries "Grid" and "Snap", WCAG 2.1.1; the walkthrough popover is not an F6 stop: F6 from it goes to the region after its anchor; the ONE Esc order of ux.md section 17 is walked by `KeyboardDrawingFlowTest`, one Esc per row), Esc from an element returns focus to the canvas container, and the shortcuts sheet lists F6, Shift+F6 and Esc with the one-line explanation under View (`KeymapSpecTest`, `KeyboardShortcutsTest`).
- The keymap freeze is gated (U8, U11, U12; D-4). Task 3.6.1c does not freeze the map until U8 (shortcut and tool-lock behaviour checked against the live Excalidraw dialog, recorded by Task 0.1.5d), U11 (screen-reader quick keys checked against the owner's reader) and U12 (stylus eraser-end and barrel availability, Spike 0.1.4) are recorded, or the owner has said "use defaults" in the batched session; with defaults the shortcuts sheet footnote reads "not verified against the live Excalidraw dialog or your screen reader" and Task 6.2.4c re-checks it before the flag release.
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-42 and UX-43 shortcuts sheet and undo/redo announcements (above); UX-22 single letters never type-switch inside text fields (above).
- Banner producer (engineering E2; Story 3.6.7). *Given* the web build and the first single-key press while `SingleKeyShortcuts` is Off, *Then* this story's dispatcher emits the `SingleKeyChip` `BannerCandidate` through `DiagnosticsPort` (ux.md banner table: "Single keys are off." [Turn on], Details and Got it under More) and never touches `BannerSlot`.
**Files**: `UI2/KeyboardShortcuts.kt`, `UI2/KeymapSpec.kt` (new; the single data table the dispatcher and the sheet both read), `JT/sketch/v2/KeyboardShortcutsTest.kt` (`// jvmTest-reason: desktop-only`), `CT/sketch/v2/KeymapSpecTest.kt` (new; layout and Cmd mapping as pure tests)
##### Task 3.6.1c: `KeymapSpec` table, `SingleKeyShortcuts` setting, non-QWERTY and Cmd mapping decisions (XS); done BEFORE 3.6.1a and 3.6.1b, the keymap is frozen only after it, and only once U8, U11 and U12 are recorded or defaulted (criterion above)
##### Task 3.6.1a: Key dispatcher + conflicts with text editing (S)
##### Task 3.6.1b: Shortcuts sheet (incl. the F6 and skip-canvas entries) + undo/redo announcements (S)
##### Task 3.6.1d: Tool shortcut scheme for 14 tools, keyboard panning, Constrain menu key and mnemonics, web default Off with hint chip and the collision note (S)

#### Story 3.6.2: Per-element semantics with a viewport cap
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Elements are individually focusable, with labels per kind.
  - *Given* a scene with `ArrowElement` from "API" to "DB" and 3000 other elements, *When* TalkBack semantics build, *Then* only viewport + selected elements emit nodes (cap 200), and the arrow reads "Arrow from API to DB" (UX-44).
- The focus ring is two-tone and tested (UX-45). *Given* the focus ring on a canvas element and on a selection handle, *Then* it is drawn as a 2 dp inner ring in the theme's `onSurface` colour inside a 2 dp outer ring in the inverse colour, so at least one tone contrasts with ANY element fill and any canvas background; each tone is measured at least 3:1 against the pixels directly beside it (the element fill on one side, the canvas on the other) in the light, dark and high-contrast themes, and the ring is never clipped or fully covered by a panel, dock or sheet (`UxA11yAcceptanceTest.ux45_focusRing_should_ContrastWithElementAndBackground_In_LightDarkAndHighContrast` renders each theme and samples both sides).
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-44 individual elements are focusable with kind labels and the >200-element statement; UX-45 two-tone focus ring (above).
**Files**: `UI2/SceneSemantics.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/SceneSemanticsTest.kt`
##### Task 3.6.2a: Description templates (exhaustive `when`) + semantics tree (M)
##### Task 3.6.2b: Tab/Shift+Tab traversal + the two-tone focus ring (2 dp + 2 dp) and its theme-sampling test (S)
##### Task 3.6.2c: "Skip canvas elements" link and F6 region cycling wired to the traversal (UX-117) (XS)

#### Story 3.6.3: Non-drag paths for new gestures (WCAG 2.5.7)
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Every drag has a single-pointer alternative.
  - *Given* a selected element and the crosshair nudge pad, *When* "Resize" then nudge right x3 at 10 units then Apply, *Then* width grows by 30; likewise move element (select, "Move element", nudge or arrow keys, Apply: x +30, bound arrows follow, one undo step; D-39), rotate, bind arrow end (pick target from list), marquee (select all in region via two taps), freehand ("draw polyline by points"), reorder layers (buttons).
- Keyboard-only drawing is a walked flow, not an implication (UX-107). *Given* only a keyboard, *When* the user tabs to the canvas, presses R (or tabs to the strip and presses Enter on Rectangle), then Enter, *Then* Crosshair mode starts at the canvas centre (or the last pointer position); arrow keys move the crosshair (Shift = 10 units), Enter places corner 1, arrow keys move, Enter places corner 2, the shape is committed and announced "Added Rectangle 3"; Enter on a selected shape opens its text editor and Esc finishes; Esc at any point cancels with no command. The tab order is the ONE list of ux.md section 10 (top bar, notice region, tool strip, canvas, action chip row, crosshair pad, selection bar, side-pane tabs and active panel, the Notices list while open, status line), fixed and tested at every layout of the chrome table; Shift+Tab reverses; Tab never traps (UX-41). The same walk is scripted for Line (Enter per point, Enter twice to finish), Arrow (Enter on a shape binds), Text, Frame, Point and Arc.
- Panning and sheet resizing have non-drag alternatives (UX-120; WCAG 2.5.7; Task 3.6.3d). *Given* the pan pad (four arrow buttons "Pan up/down/left/right" at the touch floor plus a centre "Fit"), *When* "Pan right" is tapped once, *Then* the view moves a quarter of the visible canvas width (instantly under reduced motion), holding repeats after 400 ms and then every 150 ms, a double-tap moves three quarters, and no drag is involved; *Given* the pad's footprint, *Then* it is collapsed to ONE 44 dp "Pan" button in the view cluster that opens, opaque and above the zoom cluster at the bottom-end, a plus-shaped grid of five 44 dp cells (the corner cells of the 3 x 3 box are not part of the pad and pass touches to the canvas) where the grid covers at most the share of the canvas given by the chrome table and otherwise a single row of four arrows (the variant and coverage per window are computed from the table), has NO auto-collapse timer (WCAG 2.2.1) and closes when "Pan" is activated again, on Esc or on a canvas tap, and on a side where the edge strip is active the cluster and pad are inset by the strip's gesture inset plus 44 dp so their hit areas never overlap the protected span; *Given* "Pan arrows: Auto / Always / Never", *Then* Auto shows the button only while Finger drawing is ON and a touch or pen pointer is present (whether the edge strip is on does not matter), and a mouse- or keyboard-only desktop shows nothing by default and reaches the pad from the 100% menu and the overflow menu; under reduced motion each hold repeat is an instant jump of the same step; *Given* the bottom sheet, *Then* its drag handle is also a 44 dp button "Panel size" whose tap cycles half height, full height and half, the header has [Expand] / [Collapse] and [Close] buttons, Up and Down arrows resize it with the handle focused, Esc and Back close it, and `NonDragTransformTest` completes the open-resize-close sequence with taps only.
- Freehand drag exemption (recorded, WCAG 2.5.7). Drawing a freehand stroke is a path-dependent gesture where dragging is essential, so the Freedraw tool's own stroke is exempt from the single-pointer-alternative rule; the plan nonetheless provides "Draw polyline by points" (above) so a polyline result is available without dragging, and the shortcuts/help page states the exemption.
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-40 resize, rotate, move element, bind-arrow-end, marquee, draw-polyline, layer reorder, pan and bottom-sheet resize each have a no-drag path; the Freedraw stroke is the one recorded exemption; UX-120 the pan pad and the sheet-handle button.
**Files**: `UI2/NonDragTransformMode.kt` (extends `PlacementController`/`NudgeStepper` use), `AT/sketch/v2/NonDragTransformTest.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/KeyboardDrawingFlowTest.kt` (new; UX-107)
##### Task 3.6.3a: Generic keyboard/non-drag transform layer including the "Move element" action (M)
##### Task 3.6.3b: Bind and marquee alternatives (S)
##### Task 3.6.3c: Keyboard-only drawing flow through Crosshair mode for every drawing tool, fixed tab order test, exemption note (S)
##### Task 3.6.3d: Pan pad (collapsed 44 dp button opening a grid or, where the table says a grid covers too much, a row of four arrow buttons, no auto-collapse timer, quarter-canvas step, hold-to-repeat with instant jumps under reduced motion, inset clear of an active edge strip, "Pan arrows" setting) in the view cluster, and the bottom-sheet handle-as-button with Expand/Collapse/Close buttons and arrow-key resize, with the taps-only test (S)

#### Story 3.6.4: Typed diagnostics with live region
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- `SketchDiagnostics` replaces single-string errors for v2; entries are announced as a live region (UX-48: polite by default, assertive only for an error that blocks work).
  - *Given* the `DiagnosticsPort` (Task 3.6.4a, pure, usable by `RT` without importing `UI2`), *When* `SheetSaver` raises an entry through it before any composable exists, *Then* the entry is retained and shown when the host (Task 3.6.4b) attaches.
  - *Given* a failed save, *Then* an entry "Couldn't save. Retry" with a Retry action appears and is announced once; a solver conflict adds an entry naming constraint types, never user text.
- UX criteria owned by this story, cited literally (each has a test row in validation.md): UX-48 save and solve results reach live regions, polite except for a blocked-work error.
- One generated fixture feeds every test that quotes the chrome table (engineering E2; Task 3.6.4c). The chrome table lives in ux.md, outside every source set, so `ChromeBudgetPolicyTest`, `ChromeBudgetTest`, `BannerCandidateTest` (`BannerTextBudget`), `OnboardingSequencerTest` and `TopBarTest` cannot read it from disk on wasmJs or in a Bazel sandbox. *Given* `scripts/sketch-chrome/gen-chrome-fixture.py`, *When* it runs against ux.md, *Then* it writes `ChromeTableFixture.kt` (the table text as a constant plus a content hash) which every one of those tests parses through `ChromeTableParser`; *When* the CI step runs `gen-chrome-fixture.py --check` and ux.md and the committed fixture differ by one byte, *Then* the step fails and names the regenerate command; *Given* a figure or string typed into any of those tests, *Then* a review rule rejects it (the tests assert against the parsed fixture only). The generator is the only code that reads ux.md; the table stays the single source. **Generator contract (Task 3.6.4c)**: `gen-chrome-fixture.py --ux <path> --out <path> [--check]` takes the ux.md path as a parameter (default `project_plans/excalidraw-sketch-engine/design/ux.md`, so a test can point it at a temporary copy), escapes every `$` of the table as `${'$'}` and every `"""` so the Kotlin raw string round-trips, and `--selftest` feeds it a table containing `$`, `"""` and non-ASCII text and compares the decoded constant byte for byte. A banner or toast STRING edited in ux.md is covered exactly like a figure: the fixture contains the whole block, so the CI `--check` step fails until the fixture is regenerated and every parsing test sees the new string (D-55). **Bazel wiring (Tasks 3.6.4d and 3.6.4e)**: `kmp/src/commonTest/kotlin/BUILD.bazel` documents that a target bundling `*Test.kt` files pollutes classpath-scanning guards (`AllBusinessTestsCompletenessTest`), so the fixture and the parser go in their OWN no-`@Test` targets, `sketch_chrome_fixture` (`kt_jvm_library`, `testonly`, srcs `ChromeTableFixture.kt` and `ChromeTableParser.kt` only) and `sketch_chrome_fixture_android` (`kt_android_library`, `custom_package`, same sources, compiled against `android_main` like `fake_file_system_android`), never into `common_test_fixtures`; `ChromeTableFixtureTest.kt` is added to `business_common_tests` (srcs, with `:sketch_chrome_fixture` in its deps) and registered in `AllBusinessTests`, so businessTest needs no BUILD edit; `kmp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/BUILD.bazel` and `kmp/src/androidUnitTest/kotlin/BUILD.bazel` take the dependency in Task 3.6.4e (for the `AT` and `JT` consumers only). The two commonTest consumers of the fixture, `BannerCandidateTest` (Task 3.6.4a) and `ChromeBudgetPolicyTest` (Task 3.1.4a), compile in `business_common_tests`: Task 3.6.4f adds the first and Task 3.1.4e the second to its `srcs` and to `AllBusinessTests`, so under Bazel both run in `bazel test //kmp:business_tests` and neither is an orphan source.
- The port emits `BannerCandidate`s, a pure type owned by this story (engineering E1; the candidate type used to live in `UI2`, which `DiagnosticsPort` and the W3 to W7 producers may not import). *Given* `BannerCandidate` (`V2/diagnostics`, imports nothing from `UI2`, `db` or `repository`: row id, tier, summary, short title, primary label, More actions, Details) built only by `BannerCandidate.of(row, strings, details)`, which reads the row's FIXED strings from a `BannerStringSource` port (implemented over the string resources in `UI2`) and returns `Either.Left` for any summary or short title that word-wraps to more lines than the row has at the `BannerTextBudget` line widths (the same greedy rule as `check-plan.py`), any primary label breaking the label rule, or any free-text summary parameter (there is none: names and counts go in `details`), *When* a `CT` producer that imports only `V2/diagnostics` emits all 14 rows through `DiagnosticsPort` with a fake `BannerStringSource` holding the strings of the generated chrome-table fixture (Tasks 3.6.4c and 3.6.4d), *Then* every candidate is accepted and retained, an over-budget string is refused, and `BannerTextBudget` equals the budget the chrome table computes (asserted in `ChromeBudgetPolicyTest`, so the constants cannot drift from the table). The queue that orders candidates and the slot that renders them stay in Story 3.6.7 and Story 3.1.4.
**Files**: `V2/diagnostics/SketchDiagnostics.kt`, `V2/diagnostics/DiagnosticsPort.kt`, `V2/diagnostics/BannerCandidate.kt` (new; E1), `V2/diagnostics/BannerTextBudget.kt` (new; E1), `UI2/DiagnosticsHost.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/DiagnosticsTest.kt`, `CT/sketch/v2/BannerCandidateTest.kt` (new; E1), `scripts/sketch-chrome/gen-chrome-fixture.py` (new; E2), `CT/sketch/v2/ChromeTableFixture.kt` (new, GENERATED, never edited by hand), `CT/sketch/v2/ChromeTableParser.kt` (new), `CT/sketch/v2/ChromeTableFixtureTest.kt` (new), `kmp/src/commonTest/kotlin/BUILD.bazel` (the two no-`@Test` fixture targets and the test entry), `kmp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/BUILD.bazel` and `kmp/src/androidUnitTest/kotlin/BUILD.bazel` (dependency, Task 3.6.4e), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (fixture freshness step)
##### Task 3.6.4a: `SketchDiagnostics` model, `DiagnosticsPort` and the pure `BannerCandidate` with its `BannerTextBudget` row-id constructor (all in `V2/diagnostics`, no `UI2` import; scheduled in W3 right after Task 1.2.4a, whose `SceneLimits` caps it reads, and ahead of Story 1.4.4; engineering blockers E2 and E1) (S)
##### Task 3.6.4b: Host composable + live region over the port, consuming `SketchNoticeQueue` (Story 3.6.7) (S)
##### Task 3.6.4c: Chrome-table fixture GENERATOR and its CI check: `scripts/sketch-chrome/gen-chrome-fixture.py --ux <path>` parses the `chrome-table` block of ux.md section 1 and writes `CT/sketch/v2/ChromeTableFixture.kt` (the block as a Kotlin raw-string constant, `$` escaped, so it reaches JVM, Android, iOS and wasmJs tests with no file access and no Bazel filegroup), plus `--check` mode and `--selftest`, run by a `ci.yml` step that fails when the committed fixture differs from the fixture regenerated from ux.md (S); W1, no dependencies; 3 files
##### Task 3.6.4d: Fixture PARSER and its tests and Bazel targets: `ChromeTableParser.kt` turns the constant into typed rows, `ChromeTableFixtureTest` (parse, hash, `$` round trip), the two no-`@Test` targets `sketch_chrome_fixture` and `sketch_chrome_fixture_android` in `kmp/src/commonTest/kotlin/BUILD.bazel`, the test's `business_common_tests` entry and its `AllBusinessTests` registration (S); W2, needs Task 3.6.4c; 4 files
##### Task 3.6.4e: Consumer BUILD edits: `kmp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/BUILD.bazel` and `kmp/src/androidUnitTest/kotlin/BUILD.bazel` depend on `sketch_chrome_fixture` and `sketch_chrome_fixture_android` so `ChromeBudgetTest`, `OnboardingSequencerTest` and `TopBarTest` (the `AT` and `JT` consumers) read the parsed fixture (XS); W3, needs Task 3.6.4d; 2 files
##### Task 3.6.4f: Bazel target and registration of `BannerCandidateTest` (a commonTest file that consumes the generated chrome fixture): add `dev/stapler/stelekit/sketch/v2/BannerCandidateTest.kt` to the `srcs` of `business_common_tests` in `kmp/src/commonTest/kotlin/BUILD.bazel` (that target already lists `:sketch_chrome_fixture` in its deps from Task 3.6.4d) and register the class in `AllBusinessTests` (XS); W4, needs Task 3.6.4a; 2 files

#### Story 3.6.5: "Go to element" for screen-reader users (UX U5)
**As a** screen-reader user with more than 200 elements, **I want** a searchable list of elements, **so that** I can reach any element although only 200 emit semantics nodes.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Search by label, jump, select.
  - *Given* a scene of 3,000 elements where element `e2999` is outside the viewport and its label (text or container text) is "Billing API", *When* the user opens "Go to element...", types "billing", and activates the result, *Then* the list shows the matches (label and kind only, max 50 shown, "N more" announced), the viewport pans to the element, it becomes the selection, and its semantics node is emitted and focused (live region announces "Selected: Rectangle, Billing API").
- The list itself is paged/virtualised and never builds a node per element.
**Files**: `UI2/GoToElementDialog.kt`, `V2/search/ElementLabelIndex.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/GoToElementTest.kt`
##### Task 3.6.5a: `ElementLabelIndex` (pure) + dialog + keyboard shortcut and menu entry (S)

#### Story 3.6.6: Visual accessibility, reduced motion and empty state (UX-46, UX-47, UX-74; needs 3.1.3, 3.5.2, 4.2.3)
**As a** user with low vision or motion sensitivity, **I want** readable contrast, adequate targets and no unwanted animation, **so that** the editor is usable; **and** a new user sees what to do first.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Contrast and target sizes are audited, not assumed (UX-46).
  - *Given* the v2 theme tokens and the composed semantics tree of the shell, tool strip, style, layers and constraint panels, *When* `UxA11yAcceptanceTest` walks them, *Then* every text token has at least 4.5:1 contrast against its background, every non-text token (handles, glyphs, panel strokes, focus ring) at least 3:1, every interactive node meets the `TouchTargetFloor` of the active pointer class (44 dp when a touch or pen pointer is present, 24 dp mouse-only; D-17, UX-92) and primary dock buttons are at least 48 dp; a node failing any check names itself in the failure message.
- Reduced motion replaces animation with instant changes, for EVERY transition (UX-47).
  - *Given* the accessibility animation scale is 0, *When* each of these would otherwise animate: a solve moving geometry, zoom-to-fit, a panel or bottom sheet opening, a flyout opening or closing, a toast or notice entering or leaving, the first-run coach mark appearing or being dismissed, the large-drawing or conversion banner expanding or collapsing, the erase preview fade, the selection overlay, indeterminate progress, the skeleton shimmer and the "Solving..." indicator (each becomes static text or a static glyph), *Then* the final state is applied in one frame with no interpolation (a fake animation clock sees no running animation), and every one goes through `MotionPolicy` (a grep test fails on `animate*AsState`, `AnimatedVisibility` or `Animatable` in `UI2` outside `MotionPolicy`).
- Further visual-accessibility checks, same audit test (UX-87, UX-88, UX-89). Fixes are NOT made in this story: each finding is filed against the OWNING story (tool strip 3.1.3, layers 3.5.2, style 3.5.4, constraints 4.2.3, dialogs of 5.x and 1.4.6) whose definition of done includes "UxA11yAcceptanceTest green for my surface".
  - Focus not obscured (WCAG 2.4.11). *Given* keyboard focus moves through the tool strip, panels and canvas elements at compact width with the bottom sheet and soft keyboard open, *Then* the focused node's bounds are not fully covered by any sheet, dock, banner or keyboard inset (the host scrolls or the sheet insets), and the audit fails naming the node otherwise. **Focus reveal** (UX-125; U8): *Given* the zoom cluster, pan pad, action chip row, an active edge strip, the maximum toasts and a half-height sheet are all showing, *When* `FocusRevealTest` tabs through every focusable node of every guaranteed window of the chrome table, *Then* a canvas element or handle under an overlay makes the view pan (never zoom) by the least amount that clears every overlay rectangle, the pan is not an undo entry, a control inside an overlay is never covered by another (the never-overlap list), and the audit fails naming any focused node whose centre is covered.
  - Text scaling and reflow. *Given* system font scale 200% and a 360 dp-wide window, *Then* no text is clipped or truncated without an alternative, every action stays reachable (panels scroll, the dock wraps or scrolls with edge fade, no horizontal page scroll), and primary dock buttons keep 48 dp.
  - Themes. *Given* dark mode and the platform high-contrast setting, *Then* the 4.5:1/3:1 checks hold for the dark and high-contrast token sets as well, and *Given* any default stroke colour against the canvas background in both themes, *Then* contrast is at least 3:1 (a black default stroke must not vanish on a dark canvas; the canvas colour or the default stroke adapts), with the exported image keeping the "As drawn" or "Clean" colours chosen in the export dialog.
- Empty state (UX-74).
  - *Given* a sheet with no elements, *Then* the canvas shows "Pick a tool and draw, or import an .excalidraw file" with Import and "Keyboard shortcuts" actions (the earlier "Take a tour of shortcuts" is DROPPED: there is no tour story; "Keyboard shortcuts" opens the shortcuts sheet of Story 3.6.1), both operable by keyboard and announced; *When* the first element is added, *Then* the empty state disappears and does not return until the sheet is empty again.
- Web equivalents of the text and motion criteria (UX-113; D-28). *Given* the wasmJs build in a 320 CSS px wide viewport and at 400% browser zoom, *Then* the content reflows to one column without horizontal scrolling of the page (the canvas pans internally; panels become full-width sheets), no action is lost and nothing is clipped (WCAG 1.4.10); *Given* the user-agent text-spacing override (line height 1.5x, paragraph spacing 2x, letter spacing 0.12 em, word spacing 0.16 em), *Then* no text is clipped or overlapped and no control becomes unreachable (WCAG 1.4.12); *Given* `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, *Then* the wasm actual of `MotionPolicy` reports animations disabled exactly as an Android animation scale of 0 does (UX-47). Verified in `:kmp:wasmJsBrowserTest` (Task 3.6.6d).
**Files**: `UI2/Theme/SketchTokens.kt`, `UI2/EmptyState.kt`, `UI2/MotionPolicy.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/UxA11yAcceptanceTest.kt` (new; UX-46, UX-47, UX-74, UX-87, UX-88, UX-89), `AT/sketch/v2/FocusRevealTest.kt` (new; UX-125, U8)
##### Task 3.6.6a: Token, `TouchTargetFloor` target-size, focus-not-obscured (with the overlay focus-reveal test `FocusRevealTest`), font-scale and theme AUDIT test only; fixes are filed to owning stories (S)
##### Task 3.6.6b: `MotionPolicy` honouring the accessibility animation scale, wired into every transition listed in the Reduced-motion AC, with the grep test (S)
##### Task 3.6.6c: Empty-state composable and test, shown through the `OnboardingSequencer` (XS)
##### Task 3.6.6d: Web reflow at 320 CSS px and 400% zoom (the table's degraded window 320 x 256 CSS px, no minimum canvas claimed, nothing clipped, every control reachable), text-spacing override and `prefers-reduced-motion` mapping, in `:kmp:wasmJsBrowserTest` (S)

#### Story 3.6.7: One notice queue, notice timing, onboarding sequencer, throttled live regions, touch-equivalent help (UX-90, UX-91, UX-112, UX-116; needs 3.6.4a, 3.1.3)
**As a** user on any device, **I want** notices to arrive one at a time without drowning a screen reader, and help that works without hover, **so that** nothing important is missed or unreachable on touch.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- One transient-notice queue with priority (UX-91).
  - *Given* the `AddedConstraint` toast "Constraint added.", a save-failed entry, a "Selection cleared" announcement and a conflict entry arriving within 500 ms, *When* the `SketchNoticeQueue` runs, *Then* they are shown and announced one at a time ordered Blocking (save failed, conflict) > State (read-only, other editor) > Warning (constraint conflict, capacity) > Info (toasts, undo/redo) > Onboarding (the sequencer), the tier order of the banner table; a blocking entry pre-empts and is announced assertively, the others politely; at most 3 toast notices are visible (canvas overlays, not rows) and the rest collapse into "+N more"; identical consecutive notices coalesce into one with a count. Banner-shaped items share ONE banner slot (ux.md section 1, UX-119): exactly one is visible, chosen by the rank in the ux.md banner table (the one place the order is defined; this story restates no order), and the others wait in the queue and the diagnostics list.
- Banner candidates: producers emit, the queue orders, the slot renders (engineering E2; UX-119, UX-126). A banner candidate is a `BannerCandidate` (the pure type and its budget-checking constructor are Task 3.6.4a, so every producer from W3 on can emit one without importing `UI2`); this story adds the `SketchNoticeQueue` that orders them. *Given* the producers, *Then* each only calls `DiagnosticsPort.emit(BannerCandidate)` (Story 3.6.4a) and imports no `BannerSlot`: `SaveFailed` and `SaveConflict` (Story 1.4.4), `ReadOnly` and `LeaseConflict` (Story 1.4.3), `WarningNotice` (Story 4.2.3) and `PenDetected` (Task 3.1.2e), `LargeDrawing` (Story 2.2.3), `PostConversion` and `UpgradeChip` (Story 1.4.6), `EdgePanStopped` (Task 3.1.2g), `FingerHintOn` and `FingerHintOff` (Task 3.1.2d), `CoachMark` (Task 3.6.8b) and `SingleKeyChip` (Task 3.6.1d); the onboarding ones pass through the `OnboardingSequencer` first. `BannerSlot` (Story 3.1.4) renders the head of the queue only; a `BannerOwnershipTest` (import scan) fails if a producer imports it or `BannerSlot` imports a producer, or if any file outside `V2/diagnostics` declares a `BannerCandidate`; Task 3.1.4b's "every candidate pending" test emits fake candidates for all rows of the table into the real queue and needs no producer story.
- Polite live regions are throttled.
  - *Given* 50 undo/redo or nudge events within 2 seconds, *Then* at most one polite announcement per second is emitted and the last state wins ("Undid: move rectangle" for the final one); *Given* a solver drag, *Then* the DOF status is announced only when it settles (pointer up), never per frame.
- Touch equivalent of hover tooltips (WCAG 1.4.13, UX-90).
  - *Given* any control or constraint glyph whose name or reason appears as a hover tooltip on desktop, *When* the user long-presses it on touch (or activates the "i" help affordance of a panel, or focuses it by keyboard), *Then* the same text appears as a dismissible, hoverable, persistent-until-dismissed popup and is exposed in the semantics description; disabled-with-reason items (Constrain flyout, tools unavailable) expose their reason the same way on touch.
- Notices with actions do not time out from under the user (UX-112; WCAG 2.2.1, 2.2.2; D-20). *Given* a notice that carries an action (Undo, Unlock, Switch layer, Retry, Reload, Keep mine, Open), *Then* it persists until the user acts or dismisses it, except a pure confirmation of an already-undoable edit ("Constraint added." [Undo]), which stays at least 10 seconds; the countdown pauses while the pointer hovers it or focus is inside it and restarts from the full 10 s when they leave; an Info notice with no primary action stays at least 5 seconds with the same pause; every notice that carried an action and has gone remains in the diagnostics list, and its Undo stays available as the ordinary Undo command; the durations are `SketchNoticeQueue` constants tested with virtual time (`NoticeQueueTest`).
- At most one onboarding item is visible at a time, in the ONE order of the ux.md banner table (UX-116; D-19). *Given* the finger-drawing hints, the edge-pan-stopped notice, the Upgrade chip, the first-run coach mark, the single-key chip and the empty state are all pending, *When* the `OnboardingSequencer` runs, *Then* exactly one is visible, the one with the lowest banner rank (this story restates no order; `check-plan.py` checks that the section 16 recurrence rows follow the table), except the empty state, which is not a banner and shows only while nothing else does (and again once the others are dismissed if the sheet is still empty); a lower-ranked item waits and appears after the visible one is dismissed or acted on, nothing is announced while another item is visible, and the sequencer yields to Blocking and Warning notices of the `SketchNoticeQueue`; tested with virtual time in `OnboardingSequencerTest`.
- Recurrence is defined once, per item, and is testable (UX-122; D-19; the table of ux.md section 16). The sequencer reads ONE `OnboardingPolicy` constant with a row per item: `FingerHintOn` and `FingerHintOff` are twice-or-acted (max 2 showings per device, retired early by any action button or by choosing the setting), `EdgePanStopped`, `CoachMark` and `SingleKeyChip` are once (max 1, any dismissal retires), `UpgradeChip` is until-acted per sheet (returns on every open of a `NotConverted` sheet until [Start] or the chip menu "Keep this sketch classic"; [Not now] hides it for the session only; unlimited showings), and `EmptyState` is while-empty (never counted, returns if the sheet is emptied again). *Given* each row, *When* `OnboardingPolicyTest` triggers it N times with each kind of dismissal (an action button, the close affordance, Esc, a navigation away, a new session), *Then* the shown count equals the row's rule: `FingerHintOff` shows on the first trigger, shows once more on the next trigger after an implicit dismissal and never again, and never repeats after [Turn on]; `CoachMark` shows once and never after any dismissal; `UpgradeChip` shows on every open after [Not now] and never after [Start] or Keep this sketch classic; and the policy rows equal the table (the test asserts the constant, and a second assertion compares the item list with the `OnboardingItem` enum so a new item without a row fails). The pen-detected flip is a Warning notice of the queue, once per device, outside the sequencer.
**Files**: `UI2/SketchNoticeQueue.kt`, `UI2/HelpPopup.kt`, `AT/sketch/v2/NoticeQueueTest.kt` (new; UX-91), `BT/sketch/v2/BannerOwnershipTest.kt` (new; E2), `AT/sketch/v2/TouchHelpTest.kt` (new; UX-90), `UI2/OnboardingSequencer.kt`, `UI2/OnboardingPolicy.kt` (new; UX-122), `AT/sketch/v2/OnboardingSequencerTest.kt` (new; UX-116), `AT/sketch/v2/OnboardingPolicyTest.kt` (new; UX-122)
##### Task 3.6.7a: `SketchNoticeQueue` (priority, coalescing, visible cap, the UX-112 durations with hover and focus pause) and live-region throttle with virtual-time tests, and `BannerOwnershipTest` (import scan: no producer imports `BannerSlot`, `BannerSlot` imports no producer, no `BannerCandidate` is declared outside `V2/diagnostics`) (S)
##### Task 3.6.7b: `HelpPopup` touch/keyboard equivalent of hover tooltips, wired into tool strip, flyouts and glyphs (S)
##### Task 3.6.7c: `OnboardingSequencer` (one visible item, banner-table rank order) as a priority class of `SketchNoticeQueue`, the `OnboardingPolicy` recurrence table (once, twice-or-acted, until-acted, while-empty) whose shown/acted counters `OnboardingPolicy` itself persists in per-device `Settings` keys `sketch.onboarding.<item>` (one per onboarding item, so bounded by the item list and never pruned; `OnboardingPolicyTest` asserts survival across a re-created policy, i.e. a restart, and that exactly one key per item exists) and `OnboardingSequencerTest` plus `OnboardingPolicyTest` (S)

#### Story 3.6.8: Discoverability, first run, loading states and string resources (UX-94, UX-95, UX-97, UX-109; Tasks a, b, d, e need 3.1.3, 3.6.7 and land in W8; Task c additionally needs 3.5.6, 5.1.5, 5.2.3 and 6.1.1 and lands in W12)
**As a** new user, **I want** to find every tool and see feedback while things load, **so that** nothing is hidden or looks frozen.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Tools are discoverable (UX-97).
  - *Given* a strip whose extent fits `flatExtent` (one rule for every width class, Story 3.1.4 and the chrome table; for example a 1920 x 1080 desktop), *Then* all 14 tools (select, hand, rectangle, diamond, ellipse, line, arrow, freedraw, text, image, eraser, frame, point, arc) are shown flat (no flyout needed); *Given* any other window (1280 x 800 included), *Then* each group is a SPLIT button: a main target that activates the last-used tool and a separate flyout target (a chevron, 44 dp with touch or pen, 24 dp mouse-only: the one touch floor) with a text/semantics hint "more tools", and the flyout opens by tapping or clicking the flyout target, by long-press (touch), by right-click or the stylus barrel button (pointer), AND by keyboard (Enter or Space activates the group's last-used tool; Down arrow or the context-menu key opens the flyout; arrow keys inside, Enter picks, Esc closes), so no tool is reachable only by an undiscoverable gesture. Alt+Click and Alt+drag are NOT used (UX-109): Alt+drag is the window-move gesture in KDE and other Linux window managers and Alt-clicks are intercepted there, so they would be swallowed.
  - *Given* the first time a user opens the v2 editor on a device, *Then* a dismissible coach mark (the `CoachMark` banner row, "New here? Quick tips." with [Show me], which starts a three-step Help-popup walkthrough of the strip, the zoom cluster and the overflow "Keyboard shortcuts" entry, ended by Esc or [Done] and changing nothing in the sketch) offers the three-step walkthrough of ux.md section 22 (the one place that owns the step texts; the third step points at the overflow entry that exists on every device; there is no "?" control), is shown once per device (class once of the recurrence table, UX-122), is keyboard-operable and announced, and never reappears after dismissal.
- The first-run walkthrough is specified once, in ux.md section 22 "First-run walkthrough" (U3; UX-97; Task 3.6.8e), not here. *Given* [Show me] on the coach mark or the overflow entry "Show tips", *When* the walkthrough runs, *Then* it has exactly three steps (tools, zoom, shortcuts), each a non-modal popover anchored to its target with a step indicator "Step n of 3" and the controls [Back] (disabled on step 1), [Next] (replaced by [Done] on step 3) and Esc (same as Done); focus moves ONCE to the popover container on start (its accessible name is the step-1 string, so step 1 has no separate live announcement) and to the primary button on every later step change, where exactly one polite string follows ("Step 2 of 3. Zoom. ..."); the popover is a labelled non-modal region in the tab order after its anchor, so Tab and Shift+Tab move through it and on without wrapping, F6 and Shift+F6 leave to the next or previous region with the tour still shown, Esc ends it from the popover or from anywhere (an open flyout takes the first Esc), and on end focus returns to the control that held it before (the overflow button after Show tips; the canvas region when that control was retired); the step text follows the last input device in three variants chosen when the step is shown and fixed while it stays (touch or pen without a hardware keyboard: no key names; mouse; keyboard: Shift+Tab and the arrow keys, never a key that needs canvas focus such as Home), step 1 has flat and grouped texts chosen by the strip's computed state (no collapsed variant: the Dock never collapses), step 2 names the view buttons before the pinch or scroll gesture, every text names its target and never a screen position, and never tells the user to activate the pointed-at control; Show tips also retires a visible coach mark row; the popover never covers its anchor, the focused control or its own primary button (WCAG 2.4.11) and the highlight ring holds 3:1 in the three token sets (WCAG 1.4.11); the walkthrough changes nothing in the sketch and activating the anchored control itself performs that control's action and ends the walkthrough. `CoachWalkthroughTest` asserts all of it; the anchors (tool strip, view cluster, Overflow button) are on screen in every layout, so no step ever needs an off-screen fallback.
- Loading states (UX-94). *Given* an export, a library open or import, or an embed thumbnail render that takes longer than 200 ms, *Then* a determinate or indeterminate progress indicator with the operation name and a Cancel (where cancellable) is shown and announced at start and end only; thumbnails show a neutral placeholder rather than a blank block.
- Hint strings follow the input device (UX-124). *Given* the string table, *Then* every tool has a keyboard hint and a touch hint (the touch hint contains none of Shift, Alt, Ctrl, Esc, Enter, Tab or double-click; modifiers appear as toggle chips "Square", "From centre", "Don't bind", "15 degrees" and a polyline ends with a [Done] chip, all in the action chip row of Story 3.1.4, never in the collapsible hint row), `HintTextAuditTest` fails on a touch hint that names a key, and *Given* the last pointer was touch or pen with no hardware keyboard, *Then* the touch variant is shown; a hardware keyboard attached or a key pressed switches back (`DiscoverabilityTest`).
- All new user-visible strings come from string resources (UX-95). *Given* the v2 UI package, *Then* `HardcodedStringAuditTest` fails on any literal user-visible string in `UI2` composables outside the resource table (test names, log lines and `Logger` text are exempt).
**Files**: `UI2/FirstRunCoachMark.kt`, `UI2/LoadingState.kt`, `CM/ui/sketch/v2/strings/*`, `AT/sketch/v2/DiscoverabilityTest.kt` (new; UX-97), `AT/sketch/v2/CoachWalkthroughTest.kt` (new; UX-97), `AT/sketch/v2/LoadingStatesTest.kt` (new; UX-94), `BT/sketch/v2/HardcodedStringAuditTest.kt` (new; UX-95)
##### Task 3.6.8a: Flat tools when the flat extent fits, split-button flyout targets and the keyboard path (S)
##### Task 3.6.8b: First-run coach mark with per-device store, scheduled through the `OnboardingSequencer` (XS)
##### Task 3.6.8e: Coach walkthrough: the three-step popover with Back / Next / Done, step indicator, focus management, per-step announcement and device-aware text (ux.md section 22), launched from the coach mark and the overflow entry "Show tips", with `CoachWalkthroughTest` (S); W8, in node 3.6.8
##### Task 3.6.8c: Loading states for export, library, import and embed thumbnails (S); wave W12, after 5.1.5, 5.2.3, 3.5.6 and 6.1.1 exist
